"Article 1 - PART I - THE UNION AND ITS TERRITORY
Prompt: What does Article 1 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** ""India = Bharat = Union of States"" (Indestructible Union; Territory > Union)

**Definition:**
Name and territory of the Union: Declares that India, that is Bharat, shall be a Union of States. Territory comprises: (a) Territories of the States; (b) Union territories specified in the First Schedule; (c) Such other territories as may be acquired.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: 'Union of States' was preferred by Dr. Ambedkar over 'Federation of States' because: (1) Indian federation is not the result of an agreement among states; (2) States have no right to secede from the federation. Hence, India is an indestructible union of destructible states.
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: 'Territory of India' is a wider expression than 'Union of India'. Union includes only States sharing federal power; Territory includes States, UTs, and acquired territories."	Polity::Articles
"Article 2 - PART I - THE UNION AND ITS TERRITORY
Prompt: What does Article 2 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Admission/Establishment of NEW States (External admission by Parliament)

**Definition:**
Admission or establishment of new States: Empowers Parliament by law to admit into the Union, or establish, new States on such terms and conditions as it thinks fit.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Relates to admission or establishment of new states that are NOT part of the existing territory of India (e.g. admission of Sikkim initially or acquiring foreign territory and creating a state).
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Article 2 relates to external territories becoming new States. Article 3 relates to internal readjustment/reorganisation of existing Indian States."	Polity::Articles
"Article 2A - PART I - THE UNION AND ITS TERRITORY
Prompt: What does Article 2A provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** [Omitted] Sikkim Associate State experiment (35th/36th Amdt 1975)

**Definition:**
Sikkim to be associated with the Union - [Omitted]: Inserted by 35th Amendment Act, 1974 to give Sikkim the unique status of an 'Associate State' with representation in Parliament.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Status: [Omitted / Repealed]
- Key Provisions & Context: Omitted by the 36th Constitutional Amendment Act, 1975, which made Sikkim a full-fledged 22nd State of the Indian Union and inserted Article 371F.
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Associate State status was an experiment lasting only ~1 year (1974-1975). Sikkim is now a full state under the First Schedule."	Polity::Articles
"Article 3 - PART I - THE UNION AND ITS TERRITORY
Prompt: What does Article 3 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Internal Reorganisation: ""BANO"" (Boundary, Area, Name, Organisation - Simple Majority)

**Definition:**
Formation of new States and alteration of areas, boundaries or names of existing States: Parliament may by law: (a) form a new State by separation, uniting states, or uniting parts; (b) increase area of any State; (c) diminish area of any State; (d) alter boundaries of any State; (e) alter name of any State.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Mandatory procedure: (1) Bill introduced ONLY on recommendation of President; (2) President refers bill to state legislature concerned for expressing views within specified time. Parliament is NOT bound by state legislature's views.
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Ceding Indian territory to a foreign nation CANNOT be done under Article 3; it requires a Constitutional Amendment under Article 368 (Berubari Union case, 1960). Settlement of boundary disputes without cession, however, can be done by executive action (1969 SC ruling)."	Polity::Articles
"Article 4 - PART I - THE UNION AND ITS TERRITORY
Prompt: What does Article 4 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Arts 2 & 3 laws are NOT Art 368 amendments (Simple Majority)

**Definition:**
Laws made under articles 2 and 3 to provide for the amendment of the First and the Fourth Schedules and supplemental, incidental and consequential matters: Laws passed under Article 2 or 3 for reorganisation of states and amending First/Fourth Schedules are NOT deemed to be amendments of the Constitution under Article 368.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Such laws can be passed by Parliament by a simple majority and ordinary legislative process.
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: State reorganisation bills require simple majority, not special majority under Art 368."	Polity::Articles
"Article 5 - PART II - CITIZENSHIP
Prompt: What does Article 5 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Citizenship at Commencement: Domicile + Birth/Parents/5yr Residence

**Definition:**
Citizenship at the commencement of the Constitution: Conferred citizenship on 26 January 1950 to every person who had domicile in India and: (a) was born in India; OR (b) either of whose parents was born in India; OR (c) ordinarily resident in India for >= 5 years immediately preceding commencement.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Requires two elements: (1) Domicile in India (permanent home) + (2) Any one of the three conditions (birth, parents' birth, or 5-year ordinary residence).
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Domicile alone does not confer citizenship under Art 5; it must be coupled with birth, parentage, or 5-year residence."	Polity::Articles
"Article 6 - PART II - CITIZENSHIP
Prompt: What does Article 6 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Pakistan -> India Migrants (Permit Cutoff: 19 July 1948)

**Definition:**
Rights of citizenship of certain persons who have migrated to India from Pakistan: Dealt with citizenship of migrants from Pakistan before/after 19 July 1948 (permit system introduction date).

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: If migrated before 19 July 1948: citizen if ordinarily resident since migration. If migrated on or after 19 July 1948: must register after residing for at least 6 months before applying.
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: The cutoff date in Art 6 is 19 July 1948, not 15 August 1947."	Polity::Articles
"Article 7 - PART II - CITIZENSHIP
Prompt: What does Article 7 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** India -> Pakistan Migrants (Cutoff: 1 March 1947; Resettlement exception)

**Definition:**
Rights of citizenship of certain migrants to Pakistan: A person who migrated to Pakistan from India after 1 March 1947 ceased to be an Indian citizen.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Exception: If such a person returned to India under a permit for resettlement or permanent return, they could register as a citizen after 6 months residence.
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Art 7 overrides Art 5 and 6 ('Notwithstanding anything in articles 5 and 6')."	Polity::Articles
"Article 8 - PART II - CITIZENSHIP
Prompt: What does Article 8 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** PIO Diaspora residing outside undivided India (Consular registration)

**Definition:**
Rights of citizenship of certain persons of Indian origin residing outside India: Any person who, or either of whose parents or grandparents was born in undivided India (as in 1935 Act), residing outside undivided India, can register as an Indian citizen with the diplomatic/consular representative of India.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Created specifically to cover overseas Indians (diaspora) who wished to acquire Indian citizenship at commencement.
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Registration must be done before diplomatic or consular representative of India in the country where resident."	Polity::Articles
"Article 9 - PART II - CITIZENSHIP
Prompt: What does Article 9 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Single Citizenship (""Voluntary foreign citizenship = Automatic loss of Indian citizenship"")

**Definition:**
Persons voluntarily acquiring citizenship of a foreign State not to be citizens: No person shall be a citizen of India if they have voluntarily acquired the citizenship of any foreign State.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Constitutional basis of India's single citizenship principle. The Constitution does NOT allow dual citizenship.
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Overseas Citizen of India (OCI) card is a statutory status under Citizenship Act 1955, NOT dual constitutional citizenship."	Polity::Articles
"Article 10 - PART II - CITIZENSHIP
Prompt: What does Article 10 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Continuance of citizenship rights subject to parliamentary law

**Definition:**
Continuance of the rights of citizenship: Every person who is or is deemed to be a citizen under Articles 5-9 shall continue to be a citizen, subject to any law made by Parliament.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Ensures citizenship cannot be arbitrarily taken away by executive fiat; can only be regulated or terminated by law made by Parliament.
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Citizenship rights are subject to parliamentary legislation, not executive discretion."	Polity::Articles
"Article 11 - PART II - CITIZENSHIP
Prompt: What does Article 11 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Parliamentary plenary power over citizenship (Citizenship Act 1955)

**Definition:**
Parliament to regulate the right of citizenship by law: Grants plenary power to Parliament to make any provision regarding acquisition and termination of citizenship and all matters relating thereto.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Parliament enacted the Citizenship Act, 1955 under this Article, providing 5 modes of acquisition (Birth, Descent, Registration, Naturalisation, Incorporation of Territory) and 3 modes of loss (Renunciation, Termination, Deprivation).
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Articles 5-10 only dealt with citizenship at the commencement (26 Jan 1950). All post-1950 citizenship matters are governed by Parliamentary statute under Art 11."	Polity::Articles
"Article 12 - PART III - FUNDAMENTAL RIGHTS
Prompt: What does Article 12 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** State Definition: ""GLPO"" (Govt, Legislature, Panchayats/Municipalities, Other Authorities)

**Definition:**
Definition: Defines 'State' for Part III to include: (1) Government and Parliament of India; (2) Government and Legislature of each of the States; (3) All local authorities (municipalities, panchayats, district boards); (4) Other authorities within India or under control of GoI.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: 'Other authorities' expanded by Supreme Court (Ajay Hasia, RD Shetty cases) to include bodies financially, functionally, or administratively dominated by government (e.g. LIC, ONGC, SAIL, statutory corporations). Judiciary in its administrative capacity is State; purely judicial acts are not.
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: NCERT and BCCI have been held NOT to be 'State' under Art 12 because they lack deep and pervasive state control or public/statutory monopoly."	Polity::Articles
"Article 13 - PART III - FUNDAMENTAL RIGHTS
Prompt: What does Article 13 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Judicial Review: ""Laws inconsistent with FRs are VOID"" (Eclipse, Severability, Waiver)

**Definition:**
Laws inconsistent with or in derogation of the fundamental rights. Right to Equality: Constitutional touchstone of judicial review: (1) Pre-constitutional laws inconsistent with FRs are void to the extent of inconsistency; (2) State shall not make any law taking away/abridging FRs, and any law made in contravention shall be void.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Doctrines flowing from Art 13: Doctrine of Severability, Doctrine of Eclipse (applies to pre-constitutional laws against citizens), and Doctrine of Waiver (citizens cannot waive Fundamental Rights - Behram Khurshed, Basheshar Nath cases).
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Does 'law' under Art 13 include Constitutional Amendments under Art 368? Shankari Prasad (1951) & Sajjan Singh (1965) said NO; Golak Nath (1967) said YES; 24th Amdt added Art 13(4) saying Art 13 does not apply to Art 368; Kesavananda Bharati (1973) upheld 13(4) but created the Basic Structure doctrine."	Polity::Articles
"Article 14 - PART III - FUNDAMENTAL RIGHTS
Prompt: What does Article 14 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** ""EBL (UK, Negative) + EPoL (US, Positive)"" (Reasonable Classification, Intelligible Differentia)

**Definition:**
Equality before law: The State shall not deny to any person equality before the law or the equal protection of the laws within the territory of India.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Two concepts: (1) 'Equality before law' (British origin, negative concept - absence of special privilege); (2) 'Equal protection of laws' (US origin, positive concept - equal treatment in equal circumstances). Permits reasonable classification (intelligible differentia + rational nexus). EP Royappa (1974) introduced the 'new doctrine': equality is antithetic to arbitrariness.
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: President & Governors (Art 361), foreign diplomats/sovereigns, MP/MLA parliamentary privileges (Art 105/194)."	Polity::Articles
"Article 15 - PART III - FUNDAMENTAL RIGHTS
Prompt: What does Article 15 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Non-discrimination on 5 Grounds: ""RRCSP"" (Religion, Race, Caste, Sex, Place of Birth)

**Definition:**
Prohibition of discrimination on grounds of religion, race, caste, sex or place of birth: 15(1): State shall not discriminate against any citizen on grounds ONLY of religion, race, caste, sex, place of birth or any of them. 15(2): Prohibits private discrimination in access to shops, public restaurants, hotels, public wells, tanks, roads.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Enabling clauses: 15(3) special provisions for women and children; 15(4) (1st Amdt 1951) advancement of socially and educationally backward classes (SEBCs) or SCs/STs; 15(5) (93rd Amdt 2005) reservation in educational institutions including private unaided (except minority institutions); 15(6) (103rd Amdt 2019) up to 10% EWS reservation in educational institutions.
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: 15(1) applies ONLY to citizens. Note the operative word 'ONLY' - discrimination based on religion/sex PLUS other factors (e.g. physical suitability) is permissible."	Polity::Articles
"Article 16 - PART III - FUNDAMENTAL RIGHTS
Prompt: What does Article 16 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Equal Opportunity in Public Employment: ""RRCSP + Descent + Residence"" (7 Grounds)

**Definition:**
Equality of opportunity in matters of public employment: 16(1): Equality of opportunity for all citizens in employment under State. 16(2): No citizen shall be discriminated against or ineligible on grounds ONLY of religion, race, caste, sex, descent, place of birth, residence.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Exceptions: 16(3) Parliament can prescribe residence requirement in a State/UT; 16(4) reservation for backward classes not adequately represented; 16(4A) (77th/85th Amdt) reservation in promotion with consequential seniority for SCs/STs; 16(4B) (81st Amdt) carry-forward rule exceeding 50% for unfilled reserved vacancies; 16(6) (103rd Amdt) 10% EWS reservation.
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Art 16(2) includes 'descent' and 'residence' which are NOT listed in Art 15(1). Only Parliament (not State legislatures) can prescribe residence as a condition under Art 16(3)."	Polity::Articles
"Article 17 - PART III - FUNDAMENTAL RIGHTS
Prompt: What does Article 17 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Abolition of Untouchability (Absolute Right; Civil Rights Act 1955)

**Definition:**
Abolition of Untouchability: 'Untouchability' is abolished and its practice in any form is forbidden. The enforcement of any disability arising out of 'Untouchability' shall be an offence punishable in accordance with law.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Absolute right (no exceptions). Operates against both State and private individuals. Parliament enacted Protection of Civil Rights Act, 1955 (originally Untouchability Offences Act) and SC/ST (Prevention of Atrocities) Act, 1989.
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: 'Untouchability' is NOT defined anywhere in the Constitution or the Protection of Civil Rights Act. Mysore HC (Devrajiah case) held it refers to historical social practice based on caste disability, not social boycott."	Polity::Articles
"Article 18 - PART III - FUNDAMENTAL RIGHTS
Prompt: What does Article 18 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Abolition of Titles (Military/Academic permitted; National Awards cannot be used as titles)

**Definition:**
Abolition of titles. Right to Freedom: 18(1): No title, not being a military or academic distinction, shall be conferred by State. 18(2): No citizen shall accept title from foreign State. 18(3)-(4): Non-citizens holding office of profit under State need President's consent to accept foreign titles/presents.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: National awards (Bharat Ratna, Padma awards instituted in 1954) were upheld by SC in Balaji Raghavan case (1996) as decorations, not hereditary titles of nobility. However, they CANNOT be used as suffixes or prefixes to the name (doing so forfeits the award).
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Article 18 is merely directory; it does not prescribe any criminal penalty for accepting a prohibited title. Parliament has not enacted penal law under Art 35 for Art 18."	Polity::Articles
"Article 19 - PART III - FUNDAMENTAL RIGHTS
Prompt: What does Article 19 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** 6 Freedoms: ""SAM ROP"" (Speech, Assemble, Association, Move, Reside, Profession; Citizens Only)

**Definition:**
Protection of certain rights regarding freedom of speech, etc: Guarantees 6 democratic freedoms to citizens: 19(1)(a) speech & expression; 19(1)(b) assemble peaceably and without arms; 19(1)(c) form associations/unions/co-operative societies; 19(1)(d) move freely throughout territory of India; 19(1)(e) reside and settle in any part of India; 19(1)(g) practise any profession, trade or business. [19(1)(f) property omitted by 44th Amdt].

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Freedoms are NOT absolute; subject to reasonable restrictions: 19(2) speech: 8 grounds (sovereignty & integrity, security of State, friendly relations with foreign states, public order, decency/morality, contempt of court, defamation, incitement to an offence). 'Sovereignty and integrity' added by 16th Amdt (1963).
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Available ONLY to citizens, not aliens or corporations (STC case). Right to strike is NOT a fundamental right under 19(1)(c). Freedom of Press is an implicit right under 19(1)(a) (Indian Express, Sakal Papers), not expressly mentioned."	Polity::Articles
"Article 20 - PART III - FUNDAMENTAL RIGHTS
Prompt: What does Article 20 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** 3 Criminal Safeguards: (1) No Ex-Post Facto, (2) No Double Jeopardy, (3) No Self-Incrimination

**Definition:**
Protection in respect of conviction for offences: Provides 3 critical criminal justice protections to citizens and foreigners: 20(1) Protection against ex-post facto laws (criminal laws cannot be retroactive; civil laws can); 20(2) Protection against Double Jeopardy (no person prosecuted and punished twice for same offence); 20(3) Protection against Self-Incrimination (no accused compelled to be a witness against himself).

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: 44th Amdt 1978: Article 20 CANNOT be suspended even during a National Emergency under Article 359. Selvi v State of Karnataka (2010): involuntary narco-analysis, polygraph, and brain-mapping violate Art 20(3).
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Double Jeopardy under 20(2) protects only against prosecution before a court of law/judicial tribunal; does NOT bar departmental or administrative punishment following criminal trial (or vice-versa)."	Polity::Articles
"Article 21 - PART III - FUNDAMENTAL RIGHTS
Prompt: What does Article 21 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Life & Personal Liberty: ""Due Process"" / Just, Fair, Reasonable (Maneka Gandhi; Non-suspendable)

**Definition:**
Protection of life and personal liberty: No person shall be deprived of his life or personal liberty except according to procedure established by law.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: AK Gopalan (1950) took narrow literal view. Maneka Gandhi (1978) overruled it: procedure must be 'just, fair and reasonable' (incorporating American substantive 'due process'). Art 21 expanded to include: right to live with human dignity, clean environment (MC Mehta), privacy (Puttaswamy 2017), livelihood (Olga Tellis), speedy trial (Hussainara Khatoon), legal aid, medical care (Paramanand Katara).
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Cannot be suspended during National Emergency (44th Amdt). Applies to citizens AND non-citizens."	Polity::Articles
"Article 21A - PART III - FUNDAMENTAL RIGHTS
Prompt: What does Article 21A provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Free & Compulsory Education for Ages 6-14 (86th Amdt 2002; RTE Act 2009)

**Definition:**
Right to education: The State shall provide free and compulsory education to all children of the age of six to fourteen years in such manner as the State may, by law, determine.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Inserted by the 86th Constitutional Amendment Act, 2002. Parliament enacted the Right of Children to Free and Compulsory Education (RTE) Act, 2009, operationalised on 1 April 2010.
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Applies only to children aged 6 to 14 years. Early childhood care below 6 years remains in DPSP (Art 45 amended by 86th Amdt)."	Polity::Articles
"Article 22 - PART III - FUNDAMENTAL RIGHTS
Prompt: What does Article 22 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Arrest & Preventive Detention Safeguards (24hr Magistrate; 3-Month Advisory Board)

**Definition:**
Protection against arrest and detention in certain cases. Right against Exploitation: Deals with two types of detention: (A) Punitive detention (ordinary criminal law): right to be informed of grounds, right to consult/defended by legal practitioner, right to be produced before magistrate within 24 hours (excluding journey time). (B) Preventive detention: max detention without Advisory Board review is 3 months.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Preventive detention safeguards: Detenu must be informed of grounds (except facts against public interest) and given earliest opportunity of making representation. Advisory Board consists of persons qualified to be High Court judges.
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Punitive safeguards (production in 24 hours, lawyer) do NOT apply to enemy aliens OR persons arrested under preventive detention law. India is unique in giving preventive detention constitutional sanction in peacetime."	Polity::Articles
"Article 23 - PART III - FUNDAMENTAL RIGHTS
Prompt: What does Article 23 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Prohibition of Human Trafficking, Begar, and Forced Labour

**Definition:**
Prohibition of traffic in human beings and forced labour: 23(1): Traffic in human beings and begar and other similar forms of forced labour are prohibited and any contravention shall be an offence punishable in accordance with law.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Operates against State AND private individuals. PUDR v Union of India (Asiad Workers case, 1982): paying less than minimum wage amounts to 'forced labour' violating Art 23. 23(2) allows State to impose compulsory service for public purposes (e.g. military/social service) without discrimination on religion, race, caste or class.
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Compulsory public service under 23(2) permits discrimination on ground of 'sex' (e.g. military draft for men only), as sex is omitted from the non-discrimination list in 23(2)."	Polity::Articles
"Article 24 - PART III - FUNDAMENTAL RIGHTS
Prompt: What does Article 24 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Prohibition of Child Labour in Hazardous Employment (< 14 Years)

**Definition:**
Prohibition of employment of children in factories, etc. Right to Freedom of Religion: No child below the age of fourteen years shall be employed to work in any factory or mine or engaged in any other hazardous employment.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Absolute prohibition in hazardous employment. Child and Adolescent Labour (Prohibition and Regulation) Act, 1986 (amended in 2016) banned employment of children (<14) in all occupations/processes, with narrow family enterprise exception outside school hours.
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Art 24 originally banned children only in 'hazardous' employment; the complete ban in all commercial work is a statutory development under the 2016 Act."	Polity::Articles
"Article 25 - PART III - FUNDAMENTAL RIGHTS
Prompt: What does Article 25 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Individual Religious Freedom: Conscience, Profess, Practise, Propagate (No Right to Convert)

**Definition:**
Freedom of conscience and free profession, practice and propagation of religion: 25(1): Subject to public order, morality and health and other Part III provisions, all persons are equally entitled to freedom of conscience and right freely to profess, practise and propagate religion.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Propagation does NOT include the right to forcibly convert another person (Rev Stanislaus case 1977). 25(2): State can regulate secular economic/financial/political activities associated with religion, and provide for social welfare/reform or opening Hindu religious institutions of public character to all classes/sections of Hindus.
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Explanation II specifies that the reference to Hindus includes Sikhs, Jains and Buddhists. Wearing and carrying of Kirpans is deemed included in the profession of the Sikh religion (Explanation I)."	Polity::Articles
"Article 26 - PART III - FUNDAMENTAL RIGHTS
Prompt: What does Article 26 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Denominational Religious Freedom: Establish & Maintain Religious Institutions

**Definition:**
Freedom to manage religious affairs: Subject to public order, morality and health, every religious denomination or section thereof has the right: (a) establish and maintain institutions for religious and charitable purposes; (b) manage its own affairs in matters of religion; (c) own and acquire property; (d) administer such property in accordance with law.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Protects collective religious freedom of denominations (e.g. Ramakrishna Mission, Anand Marg). 'Matters of religion' includes essential religious practices (Doctrine of Essentiality - Shirur Mutt case 1954).
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Art 25 is subject to 'all other fundamental rights'; Art 26 is subject ONLY to public order, morality, and health (does not mention other FRs). Also, right to administer property is 'in accordance with law' (State can regulate administration, unlike religious rituals)."	Polity::Articles
"Article 27 - PART III - FUNDAMENTAL RIGHTS
Prompt: What does Article 27 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Freedom from Taxes for Religious Promotion (Taxes Barred; Regulatory Fees Permitted)

**Definition:**
Freedom as to payment of taxes for promotion of any particular religion: No person shall be compelled to pay any taxes, the proceeds of which are specifically appropriated in payment of expenses for the promotion or maintenance of any particular religion or religious denomination.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Secular principle: State funds cannot be used to promote one specific religion. Prohibits levy of a TAX, but does NOT prohibit the levy of a FEE to provide secular services or regulation for pilgrims (e.g. pilgrimage regulation fee, shrine board fee).
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Prohibits taxes, not fees. Taxes collected for general public revenues can be used for cultural/secular infrastructure of all communities."	Polity::Articles
"Article 28 - PART III - FUNDAMENTAL RIGHTS
Prompt: What does Article 28 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Freedom from Religious Instruction in State-Funded Educational Institutions

**Definition:**
Freedom as to attendance at religious instruction or religious worship in certain educational institutions. Cultural and Educational Rights: 28(1): No religious instruction shall be provided in any educational institution wholly maintained out of State funds. 28(2): Exception: institutions administered by State but established under an endowment/trust requiring religious instruction.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Four categories of institutions: (1) Wholly state-maintained: religious instruction completely prohibited; (2) Administered by State under trust: permitted; (3) Recognised by State: voluntary (consent of student/guardian required); (4) Receiving state aid: voluntary (consent required).
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Religious instruction is banned only in institutions 'wholly maintained out of State funds'. In state-aided or recognised institutions, it is permitted on a voluntary basis."	Polity::Articles
"Article 29 - PART III - FUNDAMENTAL RIGHTS
Prompt: What does Article 29 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Protection of Language, Script, and Culture (Minorities + Any Section of Citizens)

**Definition:**
Protection of interests of minorities: 29(1): Any section of citizens residing in India having a distinct language, script or culture of its own has the right to conserve the same. 29(2): No citizen shall be denied admission into any educational institution maintained by State or receiving aid on grounds ONLY of religion, race, caste, language.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Scope: 29(1) uses 'any section of citizens' - Supreme Court held it protects BOTH minority and majority linguistic/cultural groups. 29(2) is an individual citizen right against discrimination in state-aided colleges.
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Article 29 heading refers to 'minorities', but text of 29(1) protects 'any section of the citizens' (including majority groups with distinct dialect/culture)."	Polity::Articles
"Article 30 - PART III - FUNDAMENTAL RIGHTS
Prompt: What does Article 30 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Minorities Right to Establish & Administer Educational Institutions (Religious & Linguistic)

**Definition:**
Right of minorities to establish and administer educational institutions: 30(1): All minorities, whether based on religion or language, shall have the right to establish and administer educational institutions of their choice. 30(1A) (44th Amdt): compulsory acquisition of minority institution property requires compensation that does not abridge this right. 30(2): State shall not discriminate in giving aid.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Available ONLY to religious and linguistic minorities (not cultural minorities). Landmark rulings: St. Stephen's (1992), TMA Pai Foundation (2002), PA Inamdar (2005) - minority institutions have administrative autonomy, but State can enforce academic standards, teacher qualifications, and syllabus.
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Art 30 rights are NOT absolute; regulatory measures for academic excellence, health, hygiene, and national security apply to minority institutions as well."	Polity::Articles
"Article 31 - PART III - FUNDAMENTAL RIGHTS
Prompt: What does Article 31 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** [Omitted] Compulsory acquisition of property (Omitted by 44th Amdt 1978 -> Art 300A)

**Definition:**
Compulsory acquisition of property - [Omitted].] Saving of Certain Laws: Originally guaranteed Right to Property as a Fundamental Right. Subject to extensive litigation and amendments (1st, 4th, 25th, 42nd).

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Status: [Omitted / Repealed]
- Key Provisions & Context: Completely repealed as a Fundamental Right by the 44th Constitutional Amendment Act, 1978. Re-inserted as a legal/constitutional right in Part XII under Article 300A.
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Property is still a constitutional right under Art 300A, but no longer a Fundamental Right (cannot approach SC directly under Art 32 for property infringement)."	Polity::Articles
"Article 31A - PART III - FUNDAMENTAL RIGHTS
Prompt: What does Article 31A provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Acquisition of Estates Protection (1st Amdt 1951 - Shield for Agrarian Land Reforms)

**Definition:**
Saving of Laws providing for acquisition of estates, etc: Inserted by 1st Amendment Act, 1951 (and widened by 4th, 17th, 44th Amdt) to protect 5 categories of agrarian reform and economic management laws from challenge under Article 14 and 19.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: 5 categories: acquisition of estates/rights; taking over management of property; amalgamation of corporations; extinguishing/modifying rights of managing agents/shareholders; extinguishing/modifying mining leases.
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: State law under 31A is protected ONLY IF it was reserved for the President's consideration and received Presidential assent."	Polity::Articles
"Article 31B - PART III - FUNDAMENTAL RIGHTS
Prompt: What does Article 31B provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Validation of Acts via Ninth Schedule (Shield from judicial review; Post-1973 reviewable)

**Definition:**
Validation of certain Acts and Regulations: Inserted by 1st Amendment, 1951: None of the Acts and Regulations specified in the Ninth Schedule shall be deemed void on ground of inconsistency with Fundamental Rights.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Created a constitutional protective umbrella. Originally 13 Acts; now 284 Acts. Landmark judgment: I.R. Coelho v State of Tamil Nadu (2007) held that Ninth Schedule immunity is NOT absolute. Any Act added to 9th Schedule AFTER 24 April 1973 (Kesavananda date) can be challenged if it violates the Basic Structure of the Constitution.
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Article 31B is independent of Article 31A. An Act invalid under 31A can still be saved if placed in 9th Schedule under 31B, subject to Coelho basic structure review."	Polity::Articles
"Article 31C - PART III - FUNDAMENTAL RIGHTS
Prompt: What does Article 31C provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Saving of Laws implementing DPSPs 39(b) & 39(c) over Arts 14 & 19 (25th Amdt 1971)

**Definition:**
Saving of laws giving effect to certain directive principles: Inserted by 25th Amdt 1971: No law giving effect to policy of State towards securing Directive Principles in Article 39(b) and 39(c) shall be void for inconsistency with Article 14 or 19.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Kesavananda Bharati (1973) upheld the protection for 39(b)-(c), but struck down the second part ('no law containing a declaration that it is for giving effect to such policy shall be called in question in any court'). 42nd Amdt tried to extend 31C protection to ALL DPSPs; Minerva Mills (1980) struck down that extension, restoring protection only for 39(b) and (c).
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Art 31C prioritises ONLY Art 39(b) and 39(c) over Art 14 and 19. It does NOT protect laws against other Fundamental Rights (e.g. Art 21)."	Polity::Articles
"Article 31D - PART III - FUNDAMENTAL RIGHTS
Prompt: What does Article 31D provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** [Omitted] Saving of laws in respect of anti-national activities (43rd Amdt 1977)

**Definition:**
Saving of laws in respect of anti-national activities - [Omitted].] Right to Constitutional Remedies: Inserted by 42nd Amendment Act, 1976 during the Emergency to shield laws against 'anti-national activities' from fundamental rights challenges.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Status: [Omitted / Repealed]
- Key Provisions & Context: Omitted by the 43rd Constitutional Amendment Act, 1977 by the Janata Government.
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Article 31D was an Emergency-era insertion that survived only for ~1 year."	Polity::Articles
"Article 32 - PART III - FUNDAMENTAL RIGHTS
Prompt: What does Article 32 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** ""Heart and Soul"" - 5 Writs: Habeas Corpus, Mandamus, Prohibition, Certiorari, Quo Warranto

**Definition:**
Remedies for enforcement of rights conferred by this Part: 32(1): Right to move Supreme Court by appropriate proceedings for enforcement of FRs. 32(2): Supreme Court has power to issue directions, orders, or writs (Habeas Corpus, Mandamus, Prohibition, Quo Warranto, Certiorari).

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Dr. Ambedkar called Article 32 'the very soul of the Constitution and the very heart of it'. Article 32 is ITSELF a Fundamental Right (cannot be refused by SC, unlike HC's discretionary jurisdiction under Art 226). Basic structure of the Constitution (Fertilizer Corporation, L. Chandra Kumar).
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Article 32 can be invoked ONLY for the enforcement of Fundamental Rights (Part III), not ordinary legal rights. In contrast, Article 226 of High Courts can be invoked for FRs AND 'for any other purpose'."	Polity::Articles
"Article 32A - PART III - FUNDAMENTAL RIGHTS
Prompt: What does Article 32A provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Article 32A: Constitutional validity of State laws not to be considered in proceedings under article 32 - [Omitted]

**Definition:**
Constitutional validity of State laws not to be considered in proceedings under article 32 - [Omitted]: Inserted by 42nd Amendment, 1976 to bar Supreme Court from considering validity of State laws under Art 32 unless Central laws were also questioned.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Status: [Omitted / Repealed]
- Key Provisions & Context: Omitted by the 43rd Constitutional Amendment Act, 1977.
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Another 42nd Amendment restriction quickly repealed post-Emergency."	Polity::Articles
"Article 33 - PART III - FUNDAMENTAL RIGHTS
Prompt: What does Article 33 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Parliament Power to Modify FRs for Armed Forces, Police, Intelligence

**Definition:**
Power of Parliament to modify the rights conferred by this Part in their application to Forces, etc: Empowers Parliament to determine by law to what extent Fundamental Rights shall be restricted or abrogated in their application to: (a) members of Armed Forces; (b) forces charged with public order (police); (c) intelligence/counter-intelligence personnel; (d) telecommunication systems for forces/intelligence.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Objective: Ensure proper discharge of duties and maintenance of discipline. Laws enacted: Army Act, Navy Act, Air Force Act, Police Forces (Restriction of Rights) Act.
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Only PARLIAMENT (not State legislatures) has the power to make laws under Article 33. Such parliamentary laws cannot be challenged on ground of contravening Part III."	Polity::Articles
"Article 34 - PART III - FUNDAMENTAL RIGHTS
Prompt: What does Article 34 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Restriction on FRs while Martial Law is in force in any area

**Definition:**
Restriction on rights conferred by this Part while martial law is in force in any area: Empowers Parliament to indemnify any person in the service of the Union or a State for acts done in connection with the maintenance or restoration of order in any area where martial law was in force, and validate any sentence passed.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Martial law means military rule invoked in extraordinary situations (war, invasion, rebellion). An Act of Indemnity passed by Parliament cannot be challenged in court on ground of violation of FRs.
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: 'Martial law' is NOT defined in the Constitution. Martial law is distinct from National Emergency under Art 352 (Martial law affects only fundamental rights and ordinary law courts; emergency affects federal structure and legislative powers)."	Polity::Articles
"Article 35 - PART III - FUNDAMENTAL RIGHTS
Prompt: What does Article 35 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Legislation to enforce Part III Fundamental Rights vests EXCLUSIVELY in Parliament

**Definition:**
Legislation to give effect to the provisions of this Part: Vests exclusive power in Parliament (to the exclusion of State legislatures) to make laws: (1) prescribing residence under Art 16(3); (2) empowering subordinate courts to issue writs under Art 32(3); (3) restricting FRs of forces under Art 33; (4) indemnifying acts under martial law under Art 34; (5) prescribing punishment for untouchability (Art 17) and forced labour (Art 23).

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Ensures uniformity throughout India in the operation and penalties for violation of Fundamental Rights.
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: State legislatures CANNOT make penal laws for Articles 17 and 23, even though police and public order are State list subjects. That power belongs exclusively to Parliament under Art 35."	Polity::Articles
"Article 36 - PART IV - DIRECTIVE PRINCIPLES OF STATE POLICY
Prompt: What does Article 36 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Definition of State for Part IV DPSPs (Identical to Article 12)

**Definition:**
Definition: States that unless the context otherwise requires, 'the State' in Part IV has the same meaning as defined in Article 12 of Part III.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Includes Government and Parliament of India, State governments and legislatures, all local authorities, and statutory/other authorities.
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Directive Principles are directed against the same 'State' authorities that are bound by Fundamental Rights."	Polity::Articles
"Article 37 - PART IV - DIRECTIVE PRINCIPLES OF STATE POLICY
Prompt: What does Article 37 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** DPSPs are Fundamental in Governance but NON-JUSTICIABLE in Courts

**Definition:**
Application of the principles contained in this Part: Declares that provisions contained in Part IV: (1) Shall NOT be enforceable by any court (non-justiciable); BUT (2) Are nevertheless fundamental in the governance of the country; and (3) It shall be the duty of the State to apply these principles in making laws.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Creates moral and political obligation on the executive and legislature. Dr. Ambedkar stated that the government cannot ignore DPSPs because it will have to answer to the electorate at election time.
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: The Constitution is founded on the bedrock of the balance between Part III and Part IV."	Polity::Articles
"Article 38 - PART IV - DIRECTIVE PRINCIPLES OF STATE POLICY
Prompt: What does Article 38 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Social Order for Promotion of Welfare of People (Minimise Inequalities - 44th Amdt)

**Definition:**
State to secure a social order for the promotion of welfare of the people: 38(1): State shall strive to promote welfare of people by securing a social order permeated by justice - social, economic and political. 38(2) (added by 44th Amdt 1978): State shall minimise inequalities in income and eliminate inequalities in status, facilities and opportunities.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Constitutional blueprint for a 'Welfare State'. Article 38(2) was the only DPSP added by the 44th Amendment Act, 1978.
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: 38(1) is original; 38(2) (inequalities in income/status) was added by 44th Amendment, 1978, NOT 42nd Amendment."	Polity::Articles
"Article 39 - PART IV - DIRECTIVE PRINCIPLES OF STATE POLICY
Prompt: What does Article 39 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Principles of Policy: Livelihood, 39(b) Material Resources, 39(c) Wealth Concentration, Equal Pay

**Definition:**
Certain principles of policy to be followed by the State: Directs State policy towards securing: 39(a) Adequate means of livelihood for all citizens; 39(b) Distribution of ownership and control of material resources to best subserve common good; 39(c) Prevention of concentration of wealth and means of production; 39(d) Equal pay for equal work for both men and women; 39(e) Health and strength of workers and children not abused; 39(f) Children given opportunities to develop in healthy manner (amended by 42nd Amdt).

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Articles 39(b) and 39(c) enjoy special constitutional supremacy over Articles 14 and 19 under Article 31C (upheld in Kesavananda Bharati). 'Material resources' includes both private and public resources (Sanjeev Coke case).
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Equal pay for equal work (Art 39(d)) is a DPSP, NOT a distinct Fundamental Right, though enforceable when linked to Art 14 (Randhir Singh case)."	Polity::Articles
"Article 39A - PART IV - DIRECTIVE PRINCIPLES OF STATE POLICY
Prompt: What does Article 39A provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Equal Justice & Free Legal Aid to Poor (42nd Amdt 1976; NALSA Act 1987)

**Definition:**
Equal justice and free legal aid: State shall secure that the operation of the legal system promotes justice on a basis of equal opportunity, and shall provide free legal aid to ensure that opportunities for securing justice are not denied to any citizen by reason of economic or other disabilities.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Inserted by 42nd Amendment Act, 1976. Led to enactment of Legal Services Authorities Act, 1987, creating NALSA, SALSA, and Lok Adalats.
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Right to free legal aid for an accused has been declared a Fundamental Right under Art 21 (Hussainara Khatoon, Khatri cases), derived from Art 39A."	Polity::Articles
"Article 40 - PART IV - DIRECTIVE PRINCIPLES OF STATE POLICY
Prompt: What does Article 40 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Organisation of Village Panchayats (Gandhian DPSP; 73rd Amdt Precursor)

**Definition:**
Organisation of village panchayats: State shall take steps to organise village panchayats and endow them with such powers and authority as may be necessary to enable them to function as units of self-government.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Gandhian directive principle. Given constitutional status and binding institutional framework through the 73rd Constitutional Amendment Act, 1992 (Part IX, Schedule 11).
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Article 40 was merely enabling/directory; mandatory 3-tier panchayats with regular elections, reservation and finance commissions came through Part IX (Art 243-243O)."	Polity::Articles
"Article 41 - PART IV - DIRECTIVE PRINCIPLES OF STATE POLICY
Prompt: What does Article 41 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Right to Work, Education, and Public Assistance in Unemployment, Old Age, Sickness

**Definition:**
Right to work, to education and to public assistance in certain cases: State shall, within limits of its economic capacity and development, make effective provision for securing right to work, education and public assistance in cases of unemployment, old age, sickness and disablement.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Socialistic principle. Constitutional basis for schemes like MGNREGA (right to work), National Social Assistance Programme (NSAP pensions), and disability relief.
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Expressly conditioned upon 'the limits of economic capacity and development of the State'."	Polity::Articles
"Article 42 - PART IV - DIRECTIVE PRINCIPLES OF STATE POLICY
Prompt: What does Article 42 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Just & Humane Work Conditions and Maternity Relief

**Definition:**
Provision for just and humane conditions of work and maternity relief: State shall make provision for securing just and humane conditions of work and for maternity relief.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Constitutional basis for the Maternity Benefit Act, 1961 (amended 2017 to provide 26 weeks paid leave), Factories Act safety norms, and occupational safety codes.
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Directly relevant to GS-II and GS-I women empowerment and labour welfare questions."	Polity::Articles
"Article 43 - PART IV - DIRECTIVE PRINCIPLES OF STATE POLICY
Prompt: What does Article 43 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Living Wage and Decent Standard of Life for Workers; Cottage Industries

**Definition:**
Living wage, etc., for workers: State shall endeavour to secure to all workers a living wage, conditions of work ensuring a decent standard of life, full enjoyment of leisure and social and cultural opportunities, and promote cottage industries on an individual or co-operative basis in rural areas.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Three wage concepts: (1) Minimum wage (bare subsistence); (2) Fair wage (above minimum, below living); (3) Living wage (highest ideal: food, clothing, housing, education, insurance). Art 43 aims for living wage.
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Promoting cottage industries is Gandhian; living wage and worker conditions are Socialistic."	Polity::Articles
"Article 43A - PART IV - DIRECTIVE PRINCIPLES OF STATE POLICY
Prompt: What does Article 43A provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Workers Participation in Management of Industries (42nd Amdt 1976)

**Definition:**
Participation of workers in management of Industries: State shall take steps, by suitable legislation or in any other way, to secure participation of workers in the management of undertakings, establishments or other organisations engaged in any industry.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Inserted by the 42nd Constitutional Amendment Act, 1976. Socialistic principle promoting industrial democracy.
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Added by 42nd Amendment, 1976 along with 39A, 48A, and 51A."	Polity::Articles
"Article 43B - PART IV - DIRECTIVE PRINCIPLES OF STATE POLICY
Prompt: What does Article 43B provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Promotion of Autonomous Co-operative Societies (97th Amdt 2011)

**Definition:**
Promotion of co-operative societies: State shall endeavour to promote voluntary formation, autonomous functioning, democratic control and professional management of co-operative societies.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Inserted by the 97th Constitutional Amendment Act, 2011. The same amendment added right to form co-operatives in Art 19(1)(c) and inserted Part IXB.
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Supreme Court in Union of India v Rajendra Shah (2021) struck down Part IXB for state co-operatives for lack of state ratification under Art 368(2), but UPHELD Article 43B as a valid DPSP."	Polity::Articles
"Article 44 - PART IV - DIRECTIVE PRINCIPLES OF STATE POLICY
Prompt: What does Article 44 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Uniform Civil Code (UCC) for Citizens throughout the Territory of India

**Definition:**
Uniform civil code for the citizens: The State shall endeavour to secure for the citizens a Uniform Civil Code throughout the territory of India.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Liberal-intellectual principle. Aims to replace diverse personal laws based on religious scriptures/customs with a common set of laws governing marriage, divorce, maintenance, inheritance, adoption, and succession. Goa is the only State with a common civil code (Portuguese Civil Code 1867); Uttarakhand enacted UCC in 2024. Supreme Court repeatedly urged UCC (Shah Bano 1985, Sarla Mudgal 1995, Shayara Bano 2017).
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Art 44 is an endeavour directed to the State, not a self-executing mandate. It deals with civil personal laws, NOT criminal laws (criminal laws and IPC/BNS are already uniform)."	Polity::Articles
"Article 45 - PART IV - DIRECTIVE PRINCIPLES OF STATE POLICY
Prompt: What does Article 45 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Early Childhood Care and Education below Age 6 (Substituted by 86th Amdt 2002)

**Definition:**
Provision for early childhood care and education to children below the age of six years: State shall endeavour to provide early childhood care and education for all children until they complete the age of six years.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Originally mandated free and compulsory education up to age 14. When 86th Amdt (2002) shifted age 6-14 to Article 21A as a Fundamental Right, it substituted Article 45 to focus on early childhood care below 6 years (Anganwadis, ICDS).
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Pre-school and under-6 care is in Art 45 (DPSP); 6-14 schooling is in Art 21A (FR); parent's duty is in Art 51A(k) (FD)."	Polity::Articles
"Article 46 - PART IV - DIRECTIVE PRINCIPLES OF STATE POLICY
Prompt: What does Article 46 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Promotion of Educational and Economic Interests of SCs, STs, and Weaker Sections

**Definition:**
Promotion of educational and economic interests of Scheduled Castes, Scheduled Tribes and other weaker sections: State shall promote with special care the educational and economic interests of the weaker sections of the people, and in particular, of the Scheduled Castes and the Scheduled Tribes, and shall protect them from social injustice and all forms of exploitation.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Constitutional foundation for scholarship schemes, tribal development programmes, and statutory safeguards for SCs and STs.
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Mentioned as the constitutional objective supporting special reservation provisions under Articles 15(4) and 16(4)."	Polity::Articles
"Article 47 - PART IV - DIRECTIVE PRINCIPLES OF STATE POLICY
Prompt: What does Article 47 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Duty to Raise Nutrition, Public Health, and Prohibit Intoxicating Drinks/Drugs

**Definition:**
Duty of the State to raise the level of nutrition and the standard of living and to improve public health: State shall regard the raising of the level of nutrition and standard of living of its people and the improvement of public health as among its primary duties. State shall endeavour to bring about prohibition of the consumption (except for medicinal purposes) of intoxicating drinks and drugs injurious to health.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Constitutional basis for liquor prohibition policies in States (e.g. Gujarat, Bihar) and national health/nutrition programmes (POSHAN Abhiyaan, NFSA, Ayushman Bharat).
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Right to health is derived as an integral part of Right to Life under Art 21, reading it with Art 47."	Polity::Articles
"Article 48 - PART IV - DIRECTIVE PRINCIPLES OF STATE POLICY
Prompt: What does Article 48 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Organisation of Agriculture & Animal Husbandry; Prohibit Cow & Calf Slaughter

**Definition:**
Organisation of agriculture and animal husbandry: State shall endeavour to organise agriculture and animal husbandry on modern and scientific lines, and shall, in particular, take steps for preserving and improving the breeds, and prohibiting the slaughter of cows and calves and other milch and draught cattle.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Gandhian and liberal-intellectual synthesis. Supreme Court (Hanif Qureshi 1958, Mirzapur Moti Kureshi 2005) upheld total ban on slaughter of cows and calves of all ages as constitutional under Art 48.
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Prohibition of cow slaughter is under Art 48 (Agriculture/Animal Husbandry), NOT under freedom of religion (Art 25)."	Polity::Articles
"Article 48A - PART IV - DIRECTIVE PRINCIPLES OF STATE POLICY
Prompt: What does Article 48A provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Protection & Improvement of Environment, Forests, and Wild Life (42nd Amdt 1976)

**Definition:**
Protection and improvement of environment and safeguarding of forests and wild life: The State shall endeavour to protect and improve the environment and to safeguard the forests and wild life of the country.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Inserted by 42nd Amendment Act, 1976. Along with 51A(g), forms the constitutional charter of environmental law in India (Wild Life Protection Act, Forest Conservation Act, Environment Protection Act 1986).
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: State's duty to protect environment is in Art 48A; Citizen's duty to protect environment is in Art 51A(g). Both inserted by 42nd Amendment, 1976."	Polity::Articles
"Article 49 - PART IV - DIRECTIVE PRINCIPLES OF STATE POLICY
Prompt: What does Article 49 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Protection of Monuments, Places, and Objects of National Importance

**Definition:**
Protection of monuments and places and objects of national importance: It shall be the obligation of the State to protect every monument or place or object of artistic or historic interest, declared by or under law made by Parliament to be of national importance, from spoliation, disfigurement, destruction, removal, disposal or export.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Enforced through the Ancient Monuments and Archaeological Sites and Remains Act, 1958 and the Archaeological Survey of India (ASI).
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Applies to monuments declared by or under law made by PARLIAMENT to be of national importance, not purely local monuments."	Polity::Articles
"Article 50 - PART IV - DIRECTIVE PRINCIPLES OF STATE POLICY
Prompt: What does Article 50 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Separation of Judiciary from Executive in the Public Services

**Definition:**
Separation of judiciary from executive: The State shall take steps to separate the judiciary from the executive in the public services of the State.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Achieved structurally via the Code of Criminal Procedure (CrPC), 1973, which separated Judicial Magistrates (subordinate to High Court) from Executive Magistrates (subordinate to State Government/DM).
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Art 50 refers to separation of judiciary from executive in public services of the State; it does NOT mean a rigid presidential-style separation of powers between executive and legislative branches."	Polity::Articles
"Article 51 - PART IV - DIRECTIVE PRINCIPLES OF STATE POLICY
Prompt: What does Article 51 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Promotion of International Peace, Security, Treaty Obligations, and Arbitration

**Definition:**
Promotion of international peace and security: State shall endeavour to: (a) promote international peace and security; (b) maintain just and honourable relations between nations; (c) foster respect for international law and treaty obligations; (d) encourage settlement of international disputes by arbitration.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Constitutional basis of Indian foreign policy (Non-Alignment, Panchsheel, UN peacekeeping, adherence to customary international law unless overridden by municipal statute).
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: International treaties signed by India are NOT self-executing into domestic law; Parliament must enact domestic enabling legislation under Article 253 to implement treaty obligations."	Polity::Articles
"Article 51A - PART IVA - FUNDAMENTAL DUTIES
Prompt: What does Article 51A provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Fundamental Duties: 11 Duties (42nd Amdt Swaran Singh Committee; 86th Amdt added 11th)

**Definition:**
Fundamental duties: Prescribes 11 Fundamental Duties for every citizen of India: (a) abide by Constitution, respect National Flag and Anthem; (b) cherish noble ideals of national struggle for freedom; (c) uphold and protect sovereignty, unity and integrity of India; (d) defend country and render national service; (e) promote harmony, spirit of common brotherhood, renounce practices derogatory to dignity of women; (f) value and preserve rich heritage of composite culture; (g) protect and improve natural environment (forests, lakes, rivers, wildlife) and compassion for living creatures; (h) develop scientific temper, humanism, spirit of inquiry and reform; (i) safeguard public property and abjure violence; (j) strive towards excellence in all spheres; (k) parent/guardian to provide education opportunities to child aged 6-14.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Inserted by 42nd Amendment Act, 1976 on recommendation of Swaran Singh Committee (which recommended 8 duties; 42nd Amdt added 10). Duty (k) added by 86th Amendment Act, 2002. Non-justiciable (no direct writ enforcement), but Verma Committee (1999) identified existing legal provisions enforcing several duties (Flag Code, Prevention of Insults to National Honour Act, IPC, Wildlife Act).
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Duties apply ONLY to citizens (not aliens). Swaran Singh Committee recommended duty to pay taxes and penal sanctions for non-compliance, but these were REJECTED and not included in the Constitution."	Polity::Articles
"Article 52 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER I. - THE EXECUTIVE
Prompt: What does Article 52 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** President of India: ""Head of the Indian State""

**Definition:**
The President of India: Establishes that there shall be a President of India, who is the Head of State, first citizen, and formal executive head.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Office of President can never be vacant; continuity is constitutionally mandatory.
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: President is Head of State (titular/de jure executive); PM is Head of Government (real/de facto executive)."	Polity::Articles
"Article 53 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER I. - THE EXECUTIVE
Prompt: What does Article 53 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Executive Power of the Union vested in President (Exercised per Constitution)

**Definition:**
Executive power of the Union is vested in the President, exercised directly or through subordinate officers. Supreme command of Defence Forces is vested in President.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: All formal executive actions of GoI are taken in President's name (Art 77).
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Military command power is regulated by parliamentary law, not personal presidential prerogative."	Polity::Articles
"Article 54 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER I. - THE EXECUTIVE
Prompt: What does Article 54 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Presidential Electoral College: Elected MPs + Elected MLAs (Delhi & Puducherry included)

**Definition:**
Election of President: President is elected by an Electoral College consisting of: (a) Elected MPs of both Houses; (b) Elected MLAs of all States (plus Delhi, Puducherry, and J&K UT).

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Only ELECTED members vote. Nominated MPs, nominated MLAs, and ALL MLCs (Legislative Councils) are excluded.
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Nominated MPs CANNOT vote in President's election, but CAN vote in President's impeachment (Art 61)."	Polity::Articles
"Article 55 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER I. - THE EXECUTIVE
Prompt: What does Article 55 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Manner of Presidential Election: PR-STV + Uniformity & Parity Formula

**Definition:**
Manner of election of President: Manner of Presidential election: Proportional representation by single transferable vote (PR-STV) and secret ballot, maintaining federal parity.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: MLA vote value = (State Pop / Elected MLAs) x (1/1000). MP vote value = Total MLA votes / Total Elected MPs. 1971 census population frozen until 2026.
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Winning candidate must secure an absolute electoral quota (>50% of valid polled votes), not a simple plurality."	Polity::Articles
"Article 56 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER I. - THE EXECUTIVE
Prompt: What does Article 56 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Term of Office of President: 5 Years from date of entering office

**Definition:**
Term of office of President is 5 years from entering office. Resignation addressed to Vice-President (who notifies Speaker LS).

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: May be removed by impeachment under Art 61 for 'violation of the Constitution'. Continues until successor enters office.
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Outgoing President continues until successor takes oath; Vice-President does NOT take over on term expiry."	Polity::Articles
"Article 57 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER I. - THE EXECUTIVE
Prompt: What does Article 57 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Eligibility for Re-election: No constitutional term limit for President

**Definition:**
Eligibility for re-election: A person holding or who has held office as President is eligible for re-election.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Indian Constitution places NO limit on the number of terms a President may serve (unlike US 22nd Amendment's 2-term cap).
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Dr. Rajendra Prasad is the only President to serve two full terms."	Polity::Articles
"Article 58 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER I. - THE EXECUTIVE
Prompt: What does Article 58 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Qualifications for President: Citizen, 35+ Years, Qualified for Lok Sabha, No Office of Profit

**Definition:**
Qualifications for election as President: Citizen of India, 35+ years of age, qualified for election as Lok Sabha MP, no office of profit.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Offices of President, VP, Governor, and Union/State Ministers are not offices of profit.
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Naturalised citizen is fully eligible in India; US Constitution requires a 'natural-born citizen'."	Polity::Articles
"Article 59 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER I. - THE EXECUTIVE
Prompt: What does Article 59 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Conditions of President Office: No House membership; Emoluments non-diminishable

**Definition:**
Conditions of President - s office: Conditions of President's office: Cannot be an MP or MLA; entitled to official residence rent-free; emoluments fixed by law.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Emoluments and allowances cannot be diminished during the President's term of office.
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: President's salary cannot be reduced even during a Financial Emergency under Article 360."	Polity::Articles
"Article 60 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER I. - THE EXECUTIVE
Prompt: What does Article 60 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Oath or Affirmation by President: ""Preserve, Protect and Defend the Constitution""

**Definition:**
Oath or affirmation by the President: Oath administered by Chief Justice of India (or senior-most SC Judge). Swears to 'preserve, protect and defend the Constitution and the law'.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Unique wording 'preserve, protect and defend' applies only to President and Governor (Art 159).
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: President's oath is in Article 60 itself, NOT in the Third Schedule."	Polity::Articles
"Article 61 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER I. - THE EXECUTIVE
Prompt: What does Article 61 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Impeachment of President: Sole ground ""Violation of Constitution""; 2/3rd TOTAL Membership

**Definition:**
Procedure for impeachment of the President: Impeachment procedure: Initiated in either House for 'violation of the Constitution'. 14 days notice signed by 1/4th members.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Passed by a special majority of NOT LESS THAN 2/3RD OF TOTAL MEMBERSHIP of each House separately.
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Requires 2/3rd of TOTAL membership (strictest majority in Constitution). Nominated MPs vote; State MLAs do not."	Polity::Articles
"Article 62 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER I. - THE EXECUTIVE
Prompt: What does Article 62 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Time of holding Presidential election: Before term expiry; Casual vacancy within 6 months

**Definition:**
Time of holding election to fill vacancy in the office of President and the term of office of person elected to fill casual vacancy: Election to fill term-expiry vacancy must be completed BEFORE expiry. Vacancy due to death/resignation/removal must be filled within 6 MONTHS.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: New President serves a full 5-year term from date of entering office.
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Vice-President acts as President only until new President is elected (within 6 months); does not serve remaining term."	Polity::Articles
"Article 63 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER I. - THE EXECUTIVE
Prompt: What does Article 63 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Vice-President of India: Second Highest Constitutional Office

**Definition:**
The Vice-President of India: There shall be a Vice-President of India, the second highest constitutional dignitary.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Modelled on the American Vice-President; acts as ex officio Chairman of Rajya Sabha.
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Draws salary as Chairman of Rajya Sabha, not as Vice-President."	Polity::Articles
"Article 64 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER I. - THE EXECUTIVE

**Definition:**
The Vice-President to be ex officio Chairman of the Council of States: Vice-President is ex officio Chairman of the Council of States (Rajya Sabha) and holds no other office of profit.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Does not discharge Chairman's duties or draw Chairman's salary while acting as President under Art 65.
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Deputy Chairman presides over Rajya Sabha when VP acts as President."	Polity::Articles
"Article 65 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER I. - THE EXECUTIVE

**Definition:**
The Vice -President to act as President or to discharge his functions during casual vacancies in the office, or during the absence, of President: Vice-President acts as President during vacancy (death/resignation/removal) or discharges functions during absence/illness.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Under President (Discharge of Functions) Act 1969, if both President & VP vacant, CJI acts as President.

Term of office of Vice-President: Term of Vice-President is 5 years. Removed by resolution of Rajya Sabha passed by EFFECTIVE majority and agreed to by Lok Sabha (simple majority).

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: 14 days notice required. No formal impeachment; Constitution specifies NO grounds for removal.
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Removal resolution can be initiated ONLY in Rajya Sabha, never in Lok Sabha."	Polity::Articles

Time of holding election to fill vacancy in the office of Vice-President and the term of office of person elected to fill casual vacancy: Election to fill term-expiry vacancy completed before expiry; vacancy by death/resignation/removal filled 'as soon as possible' for full 5-year term.

Discharge of President - s functions in other contingencies: Parliament may make provisions for discharge of President's functions in unforeseen contingencies.

"Article 71 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER I. - THE EXECUTIVE
