Matters relating to, or connected with, the election of a President or Vice-President: All doubts and disputes regarding election of President or Vice-President shall be decided exclusively by the SUPREME COURT.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Election cannot be challenged on ground of vacancy in Electoral College (Art 71(4), 11th Amdt).
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Election petition lies only in Supreme Court, after election is concluded, not before."	Polity::Articles
"Article 72 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER I. - THE EXECUTIVE
Prompt: What does Article 72 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Pardoning Power of President: Pardon, Commute, Remit, Respite, Reprieve (Wider than Governor)

**Definition:**
Power of President to grant pardons, etc., and to suspend, remit or commute sentences in certain cases: President's power to grant pardons, reprieves, respites, remissions, or commute sentences in Union offences, court-martials, and death sentences.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Pardon removes both punishment and guilt. Exercised on binding advice of Union Home Ministry/CoM. Judicial review limited (Kehar Singh, Epuru Sudhakar).
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Governor (Art 161) cannot pardon death sentences (can only suspend/remit/commute) and has no court-martial powers."	Polity::Articles
"Article 73 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER I. - THE EXECUTIVE

**Definition:**
Extent of executive power of the Union. Council of Ministers: Executive power of Union extends to matters where Parliament can make laws, and treaty rights/obligations.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: In Concurrent List, executive power normally remains with States unless Parliament explicitly assumes it.
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Co-extensive with legislative power, but subject to State executive primacy in concurrent field."	Polity::Articles
"Article 74 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER I. - THE EXECUTIVE
Prompt: What does Article 74 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Council of Ministers with PM at Head to Aid and Advise President (Advice BINDING after 44th Amdt)

**Definition:**
Council of Ministers to aid and advise President: Council of Ministers with Prime Minister at head to aid and advise President, who shall act in accordance with such advice.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: 44th Amdt: President can send back advice ONCE for reconsideration; must accept advice tendered after reconsideration. Advice tendered is immune from judicial inquiry (Art 74(2)).
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Factual basis/material underlying advice CAN be examined by courts (Bommai case 1994), though the advice itself is privileged."	Polity::Articles
"Article 75 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER I. - THE EXECUTIVE

**Definition:**
Other provisions as to Ministers. The Attorney-General for India: PM appointed by President; other Ministers on PM's advice. Total ministers capped at 15% of Lok Sabha (91st Amdt 2003). CoM collectively responsible to Lok Sabha.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Collective responsibility (Art 75(3)) is to Lok Sabha alone. Non-member can be Minister for max 6 consecutive months.
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: CoM is collectively responsible to LOK SABHA, NOT to Rajya Sabha or Parliament as a whole."	Polity::Articles
"Article 76 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER I. - THE EXECUTIVE

**Definition:**
Attorney-General for India. Conduct of Government Business: Attorney-General for India appointed by President; must be qualified as SC Judge. Chief legal adviser to GoI.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Holds office during PLEASURE of President; no fixed tenure. Has right of audience in all Indian courts. Right to speak in Parliament without right to vote (Art 88).
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Not a government servant, not in Cabinet, not barred from private practice (cannot plead against GoI)."	Polity::Articles

Conduct of business of the Government of India: Conduct of business: All executive action expressed in name of President. President makes Allocation and Transaction of Business Rules.

Composition of the Council of States: Composition of Rajya Sabha: Max 250 members - 12 nominated by President (Literature, Science, Art, Social Service) + max 238 representatives of States/UTs.

- Key Provisions & Context: Elected indirectly by elected MLAs of States using proportional representation by single transferable vote (STV).

Composition of the House of the People: Composition of Lok Sabha: Max 550 elected members (max 530 from States, max 20 from UTs). Directly elected by adult suffrage.

- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Anglo-Indian nomination in Lok Sabha no longer exists."	Polity::Articles

Readjustment after each census: Readjustment of seats and territorial constituencies after each Census by Delimitation Commission.

**Definition:**
Duration of Houses of Parliament: Duration: Rajya Sabha is permanent (never dissolved; 1/3rd members retire every 2nd year; term 6 yrs). Lok Sabha term is 5 years unless dissolved sooner.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Lok Sabha term can be extended by Parliament by law during National Emergency for 1 year at a time (max 6 months after emergency ends).

"Article 84 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER II. - PARLIAMENT

**Definition:**
Qualification for membership of Parliament: Qualifications for MP: Citizen of India, age >= 25 years for Lok Sabha, age >= 30 years for Rajya Sabha, oath before EC-authorised person.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Must possess other qualifications prescribed by Parliament under law (RPA 1951).

**Definition:**
Sessions of Parliament, prorogation and dissolution: President summons each House. Max interval between two sittings cannot exceed 6 MONTHS.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: President prorogues both Houses; dissolves Lok Sabha. Rajya Sabha cannot be dissolved.
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Sessions (Budget, Monsoon, Winter) are conventions; the only constitutional rule is that max gap between two sessions <= 6 months."	Polity::Articles

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Houses must consider any message sent by the President with all convenient dispatch.
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Right to send messages can be exercised on any pending Bill or other matter."	Polity::Articles
"Article 87 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER II. - PARLIAMENT

Special address by the President: Special address by President: At commencement of first session after each general election, and at commencement of first session of each year.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Addressed to both Houses assembled together; followed by Motion of Thanks debate and vote in both Houses.
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Defeat of Motion of Thanks in Lok Sabha amounts to a vote of no-confidence, requiring Government's resignation."	Polity::Articles
"Article 88 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER II. - PARLIAMENT
Prompt: What does Article 88 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Rights of Ministers and Attorney-General: Speak and participate in either House (NO Right to Vote)

**Definition:**
Rights of Ministers and Attorney-General as respects Houses: Rights of Ministers and Attorney-General to speak and take part in proceedings of either House, joint sittings, and parliamentary committees.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: A Minister can speak in either House, but CAN VOTE ONLY in the House of which he/she is a member. Attorney-General cannot vote at all.
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Minister who is a Rajya Sabha MP can speak in Lok Sabha, but cannot vote in Lok Sabha."	Polity::Articles

"Article 107 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER II. - PARLIAMENT

Provisions as to introduction and passing of Bills: Ordinary bill can originate in either House; must be agreed to by both Houses.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Lapse rules on dissolution of Lok Sabha: (1) Bill pending in LS lapses; (2) Bill passed by LS, pending in RS lapses; (3) Bill pending in RS not passed by LS does NOT lapse; (4) Bill passed by both, awaiting President does NOT lapse; (5) Bill where President notified joint sitting before dissolution does NOT lapse.
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Bill originating in Rajya Sabha and pending there (not yet passed by LS) does NOT lapse on LS dissolution."	Polity::Articles
"Article 108 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER II. - PARLIAMENT
Prompt: What does Article 108 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Joint Sitting of Both Houses in Certain Cases (Deadlock on Ordinary/Financial Bills; NO Joint Sitting for Money/CA Bills)

**Definition:**
Joint sitting of both Houses in certain cases: Joint sitting of both Houses in case of deadlock on an Ordinary or Financial Bill.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Grounds after 6 months deadlock: (a) Bill rejected by other House; (b) Houses disagree on amendments; (c) More than 6 months pass without passing. Summoned by President; presided by SPEAKER of Lok Sabha (or Deputy Speaker, or Deputy Chairman RS; Chairman RS NEVER presides). Decided by simple majority of all present and voting.
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Joint sitting is NOT available for Money Bills (Art 110) OR Constitutional Amendment Bills (Art 368). Only 3 joint sittings held so far (Dowry 1961, Banking 1978, POTA 2002)."	Polity::Articles
"Article 109 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER II. - PARLIAMENT
Prompt: What does Article 109 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Special Procedure in respect of Money Bills (Introduced ONLY in LS; RS has only 14 days)

**Definition:**
Special procedure in respect of Money Bills: Can be introduced ONLY in Lok Sabha, on President's recommendation. Cannot be introduced in Rajya Sabha.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Rajya Sabha has 14 DAYS to return with recommendations. Lok Sabha may accept or reject recommendations. If RS does not return within 14 days, deemed passed.
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Rajya Sabha cannot reject or amend a Money Bill; can only delay for max 14 days. No joint sitting exists."	Polity::Articles
"Article 110 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER II. - PARLIAMENT
Prompt: What does Article 110 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Definition of ""Money Bill"" (Art 110(1) matters only; Speaker Certificate is FINAL)

**Definition:**
Definition of  - Money Bills -: Definition of 'Money Bill': Contains ONLY provisions dealing with: (a) imposition/abolition/alteration of tax; (b) borrowing/guarantees by Union; (c) custody of Consolidated/Contingency Fund; (d) appropriation of moneys out of CFI; (e) declaring expenditure charged on CFI; (f) receipt/custody/audit of public accounts.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Speaker certifies whether a Bill is a Money Bill; Speaker's certificate is final. Narrow judicial review for constitutional fraud remains (Aadhaar case 2018).
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Fines, fees, or local taxes do NOT make a Bill a Money Bill. Bill must contain 'only' matters listed in Art 110."	Polity::Articles
"Article 111 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER II. - PARLIAMENT
Prompt: What does Article 111 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Assent to Bills: President may Assent, Withhold Assent, or Return for Reconsideration (Pocket/Suspensive Veto)

**Definition:**
Assent to Bills. Procedure in Financial Matters: Assent to Bills: When passed by both Houses, presented to President who may: (a) Assent; (b) Withhold assent; (c) Return for reconsideration (if not Money Bill).

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: If returned bill is passed again by both Houses with or without amendments, President MUST give assent (suspensive veto). Pocket veto = President takes no action.
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: President CANNOT return a Money Bill for reconsideration (must either assent or withhold assent). President MUST assent to Constitutional Amendment Bills (24th Amdt)."	Polity::Articles

Votes on account, votes of credit and exceptional grants.

"Article 117 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER II. - PARLIAMENT

**Definition:**
Special provisions as to financial Bills. Procedure Generally: Special provisions as to Financial Bills: (1) Financial Bill (I) [Art 117(1)]: contains Art 110 matters + other matters; introduced only in LS on President's recommendation. (2) Financial Bill (II) [Art 117(3)]: involves expenditure from CFI; can originate in either House, but needs President's recommendation for consideration.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: All Money Bills are Financial Bills, but all Financial Bills are not Money Bills. Joint sitting IS available for Financial Bills I and II.
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Financial Bill (I) introduced only in LS on recommendation; Financial Bill (II) can originate in either House, recommendation needed only at consideration stage."	Polity::Articles
"Article 118 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER II. - PARLIAMENT

Rules of procedure: Each House of Parliament may make rules for regulating its procedure and conduct of business.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Rules of Lok Sabha and Rules of Rajya Sabha are framed under this Article. Joint sittings governed by rules made by President in consultation with Chairman & Speaker.
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Parliamentary rules are framed by each House independently; cannot be questioned in court for procedural irregularity (Art 122)."	Polity::Articles
"Article 119 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER II. - PARLIAMENT

Regulation by law of procedure in Parliament in relation to financial business.

- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Ensures timely completion of budgetary processes."	Polity::Articles
"Article 120 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER II. - PARLIAMENT

Language to be used in Parliament: Hindi or English. Presiding officer may permit any member to address House in his mother tongue.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**

"Article 121 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER II. - PARLIAMENT
Prompt: What does Article 121 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Restriction on Discussion in Parliament: Conduct of Supreme Court or High Court Judge cannot be discussed

**Definition:**
Restriction on discussion in Parliament: No discussion can take place in Parliament regarding conduct of any Judge of Supreme Court or High Court in discharge of duties.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Exception: Discussion IS permitted when a motion for removal (impeachment) of the judge is under consideration.
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Absolute bar during normal sessions; opens up ONLY during impeachment proceedings under Art 124(4)."	Polity::Articles
"Article 122 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER II. - PARLIAMENT
Prompt: What does Article 122 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Courts NOT to Inquire into Proceedings of Parliament (Internal autonomy of legislature)

**Definition:**
Courts not to inquire into proceedings of Parliament: Validity of any proceedings in Parliament cannot be called in question on ground of any alleged irregularity of procedure.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Officers and MPs exercising powers under Constitution are not subject to jurisdiction of any court in respect of those powers.
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Protects against 'irregularity of procedure'; does NOT protect against substantive unconstitutionality, illegality, or gross violation of constitutional mandate (Raja Ram Pal case 2007)."	Polity::Articles
"Article 123 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER III. - LEGISLATIVE POWERS OF THE
Prompt: What does Article 123 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Ordinance-Making Power of President: Recess of either House; Max life 6 Months + 6 Weeks

**Definition:**
Power of President to promulgate Ordinances during recess of Parliament: When both Houses (or either House) not in session and President satisfied circumstances require immediate action.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Has same force and effect as an Act of Parliament. Must be laid before Parliament when it reassembles; CEASES TO OPERATE AT EXPIRATION OF 6 WEEKS from reassembly (max life = 6 months + 6 weeks). Can be withdrawn by President at any time. DC Wadhwa (1987) & Krishna Kumar Singh (2017): re-promulgation of ordinances without legislative approval is a fraud on the Constitution.
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Cannot be issued when BOTH Houses are in session (can be issued if only one House is in session). Subject to judicial review for mala fides (Cooper, Bommai cases). Cannot amend the Constitution."	Polity::Articles
"Article 124 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER IV. -  THE UNION JUDICIARY
Prompt: What does Article 124 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Establishment and Constitution of Supreme Court (CJI + 33 Judges; Collegium system; Impeachment)

**Definition:**
Establishment and constitution of the Supreme Court: Establishment and constitution of Supreme Court: CJI + other judges as Parliament prescribes (originally 8; currently 34). Appointed by President.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Collegium System evolved through Three Judges Cases: First Judges (1981 - executive primacy); Second Judges (1993 - CJI + 2 senior judges, primacy to judiciary); Third Judges (1998 - CJI + 4 senior-most judges). Qualifications: Citizen, 5 yrs HC Judge OR 10 yrs HC Advocate OR distinguished jurist. Tenure: up to 65 years. Removal (124(4)): By President after address by both Houses supported by special majority (majority of total + 2/3 present & voting) on grounds of 'proved misbehaviour or incapacity'.
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Distinguished jurist qualification exists ONLY for Supreme Court, NOT for High Court. Minimum age for SC judge is NOT prescribed in the Constitution."	Polity::Articles

Prompt: What does Article 124A provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** [Unconstitutional] National Judicial Appointments Commission (NJAC - Struck down in 2015)

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Struck down as UNCONSTITUTIONAL by 5-judge bench in Fourth Judges Case (2015) for violating judicial independence, a basic structure of the Constitution. Collegium revived.
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Articles 124A, 124B, 124C remain textually in books but are legally void/inoperative post-2015 ruling."	Polity::Articles

"Article 129 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER IV. -  THE UNION JUDICIARY
Prompt: What does Article 129 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Supreme Court to be a Court of Record (Power to punish for Contempt of Court)

**Definition:**
Supreme Court to be a court of record: Supreme Court to be a Court of Record: Has all powers of such a court including the power to punish for contempt of itself.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Court of Record means: (1) Judgments are recorded for perpetual memory and testimony (precedential value); (2) Summary power to punish for contempt. Contempt of Courts Act, 1971 defines civil and criminal contempt.
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Contempt power of SC is a constitutional power that cannot be abrogated or limited even by parliamentary legislation (Zahira Habibullah case)."	Polity::Articles

Seat of Supreme Court: Shall sit in Delhi or in such other place or places as the CJI may, with the approval of the President, appoint.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Enabling provision for circuit benches of SC. CJI has sole discretion (with President's approval); no court can direct CJI to establish a bench elsewhere.
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: No regional benches have been established so far under Art 130."	Polity::Articles
"Article 131 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER IV. -  THE UNION JUDICIARY

**Definition:**
Original jurisdiction of the Supreme Court: Exclusive original jurisdiction in federal disputes: (a) Centre vs one or more States; (b) Centre + State(s) vs other State(s); (c) State vs State.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Must involve a legal right (not purely political dispute). Exclusions: Pre-constitutional treaties/agreements (Art 131 proviso), inter-state river water disputes (Art 262), Finance Commission matters (Art 280), private citizen vs GoI.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**

Jurisdiction and powers of the Federal Court under existing law to be exercisable by the Supreme Court.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**

- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Covers matters not falling under Art 133 or 134 but exercisable by Federal Court before commencement."	Polity::Articles
"Article 136 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER IV. -  THE UNION JUDICIARY

**Definition:**
Special leave to appeal by the Supreme Court: Special Leave to Appeal (SLP): Supreme Court may, in its discretion, grant special leave to appeal from ANY judgment, decree, sentence or order in ANY cause or matter passed by ANY court or tribunal in India.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Plenary, discretionary, extraordinary jurisdiction. Can be invoked against interlocutory or final orders. Exception: Clause (2) excludes military tribunals and courts-martial.
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: SLP is a discretionary power of the SC, NOT a matter of right for the litigant. Military courts-martial are completely exempt from Art 136."	Polity::Articles
"Article 137 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER IV. -  THE UNION JUDICIARY

**Definition:**
Review of judgments or orders by the Supreme Court: SC has power to review any judgment pronounced or order made by it, subject to rules under Art 145.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Supreme Court is not bound by its own previous decisions and can depart from them (Bengal Immunity case 1955). Curative Petition evolved in Rupa Ashok Hurra (2002) after review dismissed.
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Review petition filed within 30 days; considered in chambers without oral arguments unless ordered otherwise."	Polity::Articles

- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Jurisdiction can only be enlarged, never curtailed, by Parliament."	Polity::Articles

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Must not be inconsistent with any constitutional provisions.
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Empowers Parliament to augment, not diminish, SC effectiveness."	Polity::Articles
"Article 141 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER IV. -  THE UNION JUDICIARY
Prompt: What does Article 141 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Law Declared by Supreme Court to be BINDING on All Courts within Territory of India

**Definition:**
Law declared by Supreme Court to be binding on all courts within the territory of India.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Constitutional foundation of the Doctrine of Precedent (stare decisis). 'Law declared' includes ratio decidendi and considered obiter dicta. SC itself is NOT bound by its own rulings.
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Binding on ALL courts in India, but does NOT bind the Supreme Court itself."	Polity::Articles
"Article 142 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER IV. -  THE UNION JUDICIARY
Prompt: What does Article 142 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Enforcement of Decrees & Orders of SC: Power to pass orders for ""Complete Justice""

**Definition:**
Enforcement of decrees and orders of the Supreme Court and orders as to discovery, etc: Enforcement of decrees and orders of Supreme Court and orders as to discovery, etc.: SC may pass such decree or order as is necessary for doing COMPLETE JUSTICE in any cause or matter.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Extraordinary inherent equitable power. Used in Bhopal Gas Tragedy, Ayodhya Title dispute, dissolving irretrievably broken marriages, Vishaka guidelines, environmental protection.
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Art 142 cannot be used to bypass express statutory prohibitions or basic structure (Supreme Court Bar Association v Union of India 1998)."	Polity::Articles
"Article 143 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER IV. -  THE UNION JUDICIARY
Prompt: What does Article 143 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Power of President to Consult Supreme Court (Advisory Jurisdiction; Consultative & non-binding)

**Definition:**
Power of the President to consult the Supreme Court: Power of President to consult Supreme Court (Advisory Jurisdiction): President may refer question of law or fact of public importance for SC opinion.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Two categories: (1) Art 143(1): question of public importance - SC MAY give opinion or refuse (refused in Ayodhya reference 1993); (2) Art 143(2): pre-constitutional treaties/disputes - SC MUST give opinion (mandatory).
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Opinion given under Art 143 is ADVISORY ONLY and NOT binding on the President; it is also not 'law declared' under Art 141, though entitled to highest respect."	Polity::Articles
"Article 144 - PART V - THE UNION | CHAPTER IV. -  THE UNION JUDICIARY

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Ensures universal enforceability of Supreme Court judgments throughout India.
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Binding on executive and administrative authorities to execute SC orders."	Polity::Articles

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**

"Article 155 - PART VI - THE STATES | CHAPTER II. -  THE EXECUTIVE

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: No security of tenure and no fixed grounds for removal in Constitution. BP Singhal case (2010): Governor cannot be removed arbitrarily or merely because Central government changes; removal is subject to judicial review for mala fides.
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Governor has NO fixed tenure; holds office entirely during the pleasure of the President."	Polity::Articles

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**

**Definition:**

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: 163(1): Governor has constitutional DISCRETION, unlike President (Art 74 has no discretion clause). 163(2): If any question arises whether a matter is in Governor's discretion, GOVERNOR'S DECISION IS FINAL. 163(3): Advice tendered by Ministers cannot be inquired into by courts.
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Governor has explicit constitutional discretion (Art 163); President has NO explicit textual discretion under Art 74. Discretion must be exercised reasonably, not arbitrarily (Nabam Rebia case 2016)."	Polity::Articles

"Article 226 - PART VI - THE STATES | CHAPTER V. - THE HIGH COURTS IN THE
Prompt: What does Article 226 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Writ Jurisdiction of High Courts: Wider than Art 32 (Enforcement of FRs + ""Any Other Purpose"")

**Definition:**
Power of High Courts to issue certain writs: HC has power throughout its territorial jurisdiction to issue directions, orders or writs (Habeas Corpus, Mandamus, Prohibition, Quo Warranto, Certiorari) for enforcement of Fundamental Rights AND FOR ANY OTHER PURPOSE.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: CRITICAL COMPARISON WITH ART 32: (1) Art 226 is WIDER than Art 32 because it extends to FRs AND ordinary legal rights ('for any other purpose'); (2) Art 32 is itself a Fundamental Right (SC cannot refuse), whereas Art 226 is a discretionary constitutional remedy (HC may decline if alternative remedy exists); (3) Art 226(2) allows HC to issue writs outside its territory if CAUSE OF ACTION arises within territory. Basic structure (Chandra Kumar 1997).
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Art 226 is wider in scope than Art 32, but narrower in territorial reach (SC reaches all of India). Art 226 is discretionary; Art 32 is a guaranteed fundamental right."	Polity::Articles

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: E.g. Calcutta HC over Andaman & Nicobar; Kerala HC over Lakshadweep; Bombay HC over Dadra & Nagar Haveli and Daman & Diu; Punjab & Haryana HC over Chandigarh.
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Delhi and J&K are the only UTs with their own independent High Courts."	Polity::Articles

**Definition:**

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: E.g. Bombay HC (Maharashtra & Goa); Punjab and Haryana HC (Punjab & Haryana & Chandigarh); Guwahati HC (Assam, Nagaland, Mizoram, Arunachal Pradesh).

"Article 243A - PART IX - THE PANCHAYATS

**Definition:**
Gram Sabha: A body consisting of persons registered in the electoral rolls relating to a village comprised within the area of Panchayat at the village level.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Foundation of Panchayati Raj. Exercises powers and performs functions at village level as State Legislature provides by law.
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Gram Sabha includes ALL registered voters of the village, not just household heads or elected representatives."	Polity::Articles
"Article 243B - PART IX - THE PANCHAYATS
Prompt: What does Article 243B provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Constitution of Panchayats: Mandatory 3-Tier System (Exemption for intermediate tier if pop < 20 Lakhs)

**Definition:**
Constitution of Panchayats: 3-tier system in every State - Village level, Intermediate level (block/taluka), and District level (Zilla).

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Proviso: Panchayats at intermediate level MAY NOT BE CONSTITUTED in a State having a population NOT EXCEEDING 20 LAKHS.
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: 3-tier structure is mandatory, but intermediate tier is optional for States with population <= 20 lakhs."	Polity::Articles
"Article 243C - PART IX - THE PANCHAYATS
Prompt: What does Article 243C provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Composition of Panchayats: Direct election for members; Chairperson election (direct/indirect)

**Definition:**
Composition of Panchayats: All seats filled by direct election from territorial constituencies. Ratio between population and seats must be uniform.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Chairperson of village panchayat elected as State law provides (direct or indirect); Chairperson of intermediate and district panchayats MUST BE ELECTED INDIRECTLY from amongst elected members.
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Chairperson of Intermediate and District Panchayats MUST be elected indirectly; Village Chairperson mode is left to State law."	Polity::Articles
"Article 243D - PART IX - THE PANCHAYATS
Prompt: What does Article 243D provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Reservation in Panchayats: SC/ST proportional reservation + 1/3rd MANDATORY RESERVATION FOR WOMEN

**Definition:**
Reservation of seats in Panchayats: (1) SCs and STs: reserved in proportion to their population; (2) Women: NOT LESS THAN ONE-THIRD (33%) of total seats reserved for women (including SC/ST women); (3) Offices of Chairpersons reserved for SCs/STs and women (min 1/3rd).

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Many States (Bihar, MP, Rajasthan, Kerala, etc.) have increased women's reservation to 50% by state laws. State legislature may also provide reservation for backward classes (OBCs).
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: 1/3rd reservation for women in Panchayats is a MANDATORY constitutional minimum; it applies to both ordinary seats AND chairperson offices."	Polity::Articles
"Article 243E - PART IX - THE PANCHAYATS
Prompt: What does Article 243E provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Duration of Panchayats: 5 Years; Elections within 6 months of dissolution; Remainder term rule

**Definition:**
Duration of Panchayats, etc: Duration of Panchayats: 5 years from date of first meeting. Elections must be completed: (a) before expiry of 5-year term; (b) before expiration of 6 MONTHS from dissolution.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: A Panchayat reconstituted upon premature dissolution serves ONLY FOR THE REMAINDER of the period for which the dissolved Panchayat would have continued.
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Dissolved Panchayat reconstituted does NOT get a fresh 5-year term; it serves only the remainder of the 5-year period (unless remainder is <6 months, in which case election is skipped)."	Polity::Articles

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**

- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Fiscal powers are purely statutory."	Polity::Articles
"Article 243Y - PART IXA - THE MUNICIPALITIES

"Article 245 - PART XI - RELATIONS BETWEEN THE UNION AND THE STATES | CHAPTER I. - LEGISLATIVE RELATIONS
Prompt: What does Article 245 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Extent of Laws made by Parliament and State Legislatures (Parliament has Extra-Territorial power)

**Definition:**
Extent of laws made by Parliament and by the Legislatures of States: Parliament may make laws for whole or any part of India (and has EXTRA-TERRITORIAL LEGISLATION power). State Legislature can make laws only for whole or part of the State.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Extra-territorial legislation applies to Indian citizens and property anywhere in the world; State laws require 'territorial nexus' (State of Bombay v RMD Chamarbaugwala).
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Only Parliament has extra-territorial legislative competence; State laws cannot operate extra-territorially without territorial nexus."	Polity::Articles
"Article 246 - PART XI - RELATIONS BETWEEN THE UNION AND THE STATES | CHAPTER I. - LEGISLATIVE RELATIONS
Prompt: What does Article 246 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Subject-Matter of Laws: Seventh Schedule (List I Union, List II State, List III Concurrent)

**Definition:**
Subject-matter of laws made by Parliament and by the Legislatures of States: Subject-matter of laws made by Parliament and State Legislatures (Seventh Schedule): List I (Union List - Parliament exclusive), List II (State List - State exclusive), List III (Concurrent List - both Parliament and States).

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Federal supremacy: In case of overlapping, Union List prevails over State and Concurrent Lists; Concurrent List prevails over State List.
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: For Union Territories, Parliament can make laws on ANY subject in ALL 3 lists (including State List) under Art 246(4)."	Polity::Articles
"Article 246A - PART XI - RELATIONS BETWEEN THE UNION AND THE STATES | CHAPTER I. - LEGISLATIVE RELATIONS

**Definition:**
Special provision with respect to goods and services tax: Special provision with respect to Goods and Services Tax (GST): Inserted by 101st Constitutional Amendment Act, 2016.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Confers SIMULTANEOUS LEGISLATIVE POWER on Parliament and State Legislatures to make laws regarding GST. Parliament has exclusive power over inter-state trade/commerce GST (IGST under Art 269A).
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: GST Council recommendations are persuasive, not binding mandates)."	Polity::Articles
"Article 247 - PART XI - RELATIONS BETWEEN THE UNION AND THE STATES | CHAPTER I. - LEGISLATIVE RELATIONS

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Enables creation of special central courts/tribunals for Union List statutes.

"Article 248 - PART XI - RELATIONS BETWEEN THE UNION AND THE STATES | CHAPTER I. - LEGISLATIVE RELATIONS
Prompt: What does Article 248 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Residuary Powers of Legislation: Vests EXCLUSIVELY in Parliament (Including residuary taxation)

**Definition:**
Residuary powers of legislation: Parliament has exclusive power to make any law with respect to ANY MATTER NOT ENUMERATED in Concurrent List or State List.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Includes power of imposing a tax not mentioned in either list. In India, residuary power belongs to the CENTRE (modelled on Canada), unlike USA and Australia where residuary powers belong to States.
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Residuary powers belong to PARLIAMENT in India, not to the States."	Polity::Articles
"Article 249 - PART XI - RELATIONS BETWEEN THE UNION AND THE STATES | CHAPTER I. - LEGISLATIVE RELATIONS
Prompt: What does Article 249 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Power of Parliament to Legislate on State List in National Interest (Rajya Sabha 2/3rd Present & Voting Resolution)

**Definition:**
Power of Parliament to legislate with respect to a matter in the State List in the national interest: Power of Parliament to legislate with respect to a matter in the State List in the NATIONAL INTEREST.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: CONDITIONS: RAJYA SABHA must pass a resolution supported by NOT LESS THAN 2/3RD OF MEMBERS PRESENT AND VOTING. Resolution remains in force for max 1 YEAR (can be renewed for 1 yr at a time). Law ceases to have effect 6 MONTHS after resolution expires.
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Resolution can be passed ONLY by Rajya Sabha (federal safeguard), by 2/3rd present and voting. State Legislature can still make law on same subject, but parliamentary law prevails in inconsistency (Art 251)."	Polity::Articles
"Article 250 - PART XI - RELATIONS BETWEEN THE UNION AND THE STATES | CHAPTER I. - LEGISLATIVE RELATIONS
Prompt: What does Article 250 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Power of Parliament to Legislate on State List during National Emergency (Ceases 6 months after emergency)

**Definition:**
Power of Parliament to legislate with respect to any matter in the State List if a Proclamation of Emergency is in operation: Power of Parliament to legislate on State List matters if a PROCLAMATION OF EMERGENCY (Art 352) is in operation.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Applies automatically during National Emergency. Law ceases to operate 6 MONTHS after emergency ceases.
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: State legislature is NOT suspended; it can still pass laws, but Union law overrides state law in case of conflict (Art 251)."	Polity::Articles
"Article 251 - PART XI - RELATIONS BETWEEN THE UNION AND THE STATES | CHAPTER I. - LEGISLATIVE RELATIONS
Prompt: What does Article 251 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Inconsistency between Parliamentary Laws under Arts 249/250 and State Laws (Parliamentary law prevails)

**Definition:**
Inconsistency between laws made by Parliament under articles 249 and 250 and laws made by the Legislatures of States: Inconsistency between laws made by Parliament under Articles 249 and 250 and laws made by State Legislatures: Parliamentary law prevails; State law void to extent of repugnancy, but only so long as parliamentary law remains in force.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: State law remains in abeyance during operational period and revives thereafter.
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Governs supremacy specifically for Arts 249 and 250, distinct from general Art 254 repugnancy."	Polity::Articles
"Article 252 - PART XI - RELATIONS BETWEEN THE UNION AND THE STATES | CHAPTER I. - LEGISLATIVE RELATIONS
Prompt: What does Article 252 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Power of Parliament to Legislate for Two or More States by Consent (Resolutions by State Assemblies)

**Definition:**
Power of Parliament to legislate for two or more States by consent and adoption of such legislation by any other State: Power of Parliament to legislate for two or more States by CONSENT and adoption of such legislation by any other State.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: If Legislatures of 2 OR MORE STATES pass resolutions requesting Parliament to legislate on a State List subject, Parliament can pass such Act. Act applies to consenting States and any other State that adopts it later. CRITICAL: SUCH ACT CAN BE AMENDED OR REPEALED ONLY BY PARLIAMENT, NOT BY STATE LEGISLATURES.
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Once States surrender legislative competence under Art 252, they CANNOT amend or repeal that Act; only Parliament can amend or repeal it (e.g. Wildlife Protection Act 1972, Water Pollution Act 1974)."	Polity::Articles
"Article 253 - PART XI - RELATIONS BETWEEN THE UNION AND THE STATES | CHAPTER I. - LEGISLATIVE RELATIONS
Prompt: What does Article 253 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Legislation for Giving Effect to International Agreements (Parliament can legislate on ANY state subject)

**Definition:**
Legislation for giving effect to international agreements: Parliament has power to make ANY law for whole or part of India for implementing any treaty, agreement or convention with foreign countries or decisions of international conferences.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Enables Parliament to legislate on STATE LIST subjects without consulting States if needed to implement international treaties (e.g. TRIPS patent laws, environmental conventions).
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Treaty implementation allows Parliament to override the State List completely without any State consent."	Polity::Articles
"Article 254 - PART XI - RELATIONS BETWEEN THE UNION AND THE STATES | CHAPTER I. - LEGISLATIVE RELATIONS
Prompt: What does Article 254 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Inconsistency between Parliamentary Laws and State Laws (Concurrent List Repugnancy; Presidential Assent exception)

**Definition:**
Inconsistency between laws made by Parliament and laws made by the Legislatures of States: Inconsistency between laws made by Parliament and laws made by State Legislatures (Doctrine of Repugnancy): In Concurrent List, parliamentary law prevails over state law.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: CRITICAL EXCEPTION (Art 254(2)): If a State law on a Concurrent subject is repugnant to an earlier Parliamentary law, the State law SHALL PREVAIL IN THAT STATE IF IT HAS BEEN RESERVED FOR PRESIDENT'S CONSIDERATION AND RECEIVED PRESIDENTIAL ASSENT. Proviso: Parliament can at any time enact fresh law adding to, amending, varying or repealing such State law.
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Repugnancy under Art 254 applies ONLY to Concurrent List (List III) matters (Hoechst case). State law can override central law in that State if it received President's assent under 254(2)."	Polity::Articles

"Article 270 - PART XII - FINANCE, PROPERTY, CONTRACTS AND SUITS | CHAPTER I. - FINANCE
Prompt: What does Article 270 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Taxes Levied and Distributed between Union and States (Divisible Pool based on Finance Commission)

Prompt: What does Article 279A provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Goods and Services Tax (GST) Council: 1/3rd Centre + 2/3rd States weighted voting; 3/4th majority for decisions

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: COMPOSITION: Union Finance Minister (Chairperson), Union MoS Finance, and State Finance/Taxation Ministers. VOTING FORMULA: Central Government vote weight = 1/3RD (33.3%); All State Governments together vote weight = 2/3RD (66.7%). Decision requires a majority of NOT LESS THAN 3/4TH (75%) OF WEIGHTED VOTES of members present and voting. Quorum = 1/2 members.
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Centre has veto power (holds 33.3%, while 75% needed); States collectively also have veto power (hold 66.7%). Supreme Court in Mohit Minerals (2022) held GST Council recommendations have persuasive value, not legally binding on legislatures."	Polity::Articles

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Qualifications determined by Parliament (Finance Commission Act 1951: Judge of HC, specialised finance/accounts, public affairs, economics). DUTIES: (a) Distribution of net tax proceeds between Union and States (vertical devolution) and allocation between States (horizontal devolution); (b) Principles governing grants-in-aid under Art 275; (c) Measures to augment State Consolidated Fund to supplement Panchayat/Municipality resources based on SFC recommendations.
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Recommendations of the Finance Commission are ADVISORY in nature; not legally binding on Government (though accepted by convention). First FC chaired by KC Neogy; 15th FC chaired by NK Singh; 16th FC chaired by Arvind Panagariya."	Polity::Articles

"Article 300 - PART XII - FINANCE, PROPERTY, CONTRACTS AND SUITS | CHAPTER III. -  PROPERTY, CONTRACTS, RIGHTS, LIABILITIES,
Prompt: What does Article 300 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Suits and Proceedings: Government of India may sue or be sued by Name of ""Union of India"" (States by State name)

**Definition:**
Suits and proceedings: Government of India may sue or be sued by the name of the Union of India; State Government by name of the State.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Sovereign Immunity doctrine: Historically distinguished sovereign functions (defence, justice) from non-sovereign/commercial functions (Kasturi Lal case 1965). However, Supreme Court has severely curtailed sovereign immunity in fundamental rights violations, awarding compensation under Art 32/226 (Rudul Sah, Nilabati Behera cases).
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: 'Union of India' or 'State of [Name]'."	Polity::Articles
"Article 300A - PART XII - FINANCE, PROPERTY, CONTRACTS AND SUITS | CHAPTER IV. -  RIGHT TO PROPERTY
Prompt: What does Article 300A provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Right to Property: Legal and Constitutional Right, NOT a Fundamental Right (44th Amdt 1978)

**Definition:**
Persons not to be deprived of property save by authority of law: No person shall be deprived of his property save by authority of law.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Inserted by the 44th Constitutional Amendment Act, 1978 after repealing Article 19(1)(f) and Article 31. Property is now a CONSTITUTIONAL/LEGAL RIGHT, NOT A FUNDAMENTAL RIGHT. Applies to citizens and non-citizens. Executive cannot seize property without statutory backing (Vidya Devi v State of HP 2020: state cannot grab private property without paying compensation).
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Violation of Art 300A cannot be challenged directly in Supreme Court under Art 32 (since it is in Part XII, not Part III); remedy lies in High Court under Art 226 or civil suit."	Polity::Articles
"Article 301 - PART XIII - TRADE, COMMERCE AND INTERCOURSE WITHIN THE TERRITORY OF INDIA
Prompt: What does Article 301 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Freedom of Trade, Commerce, and Intercourse throughout the Territory of India

**Definition:**
Freedom of trade, commerce and intercourse: Subject to other provisions of this Part, trade, commerce and intercourse throughout territory of India shall be FREE.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Modelled on Section 92 of Australian Constitution. Covers both inter-State and intra-State trade. Prohibits fiscal and physical barriers to trade.
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Art 301 is not a Fundamental Right (found in Part XIII), but an independent constitutional limitation on legislative power."	Polity::Articles
"Article 302 - PART XIII - TRADE, COMMERCE AND INTERCOURSE WITHIN THE TERRITORY OF INDIA
Prompt: What does Article 302 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Power of Parliament to Impose Restrictions on Trade, Commerce, and Intercourse in Public Interest

**Definition:**
Power of Parliament to impose restrictions on trade, commerce and intercourse in the PUBLIC INTEREST.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Parliament can enact laws like Essential Commodities Act restricting movement of goods.
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Parliament can impose restrictions by ordinary law in public interest."	Polity::Articles
"Article 303 - PART XIII - TRADE, COMMERCE AND INTERCOURSE WITHIN THE TERRITORY OF INDIA
Prompt: What does Article 303 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Restrictions on Legislative Powers of Union and States with regard to Trade (No discriminatory preference)

**Definition:**
and intercourse. Restrictions on the legislative powers of the Union and of the States with regard to trade and commerce: Restrictions on legislative powers of Union and States regarding trade: Neither Parliament nor State Legislature can make law giving preference to one State over another or discriminating between States.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Clause (2) allows Parliament to discriminate ONLY for dealing with a situation arising from SCARCITY OF GOODS in any part of India.
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Non-discrimination is the rule; scarcity of goods is the sole exception allowing central preference."	Polity::Articles
"Article 304 - PART XIII - TRADE, COMMERCE AND INTERCOURSE WITHIN THE TERRITORY OF INDIA

**Definition:**
Restrictions on trade, commerce and intercourse among States by State Legislatures: (a) State can impose non-discriminatory taxes on goods imported from other States; (b) State can impose reasonable restrictions in public interest.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: CRITICAL PROVISO to 304(b): No Bill for imposing restrictions under 304(b) shall be introduced in State Legislature WITHOUT PREVIOUS SANCTION OF THE PRESIDENT (Atiabari Tea Co, Automobile Transport cases).

"Article 310 - PART XIV - SERVICES UNDER THE UNION AND THE STATES | CHAPTER I. -  SERVICES

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**

"Article 311 - PART XIV - SERVICES UNDER THE UNION AND THE STATES | CHAPTER I. -  SERVICES
Prompt: What does Article 311 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Constitutional Safeguards for Civil Servants: No dismissal by subordinate authority; Inquiry & Reasonable Opportunity

**Definition:**
Dismissal, removal or reduction in rank of persons employed in civil capacities under the Union or a State: Dismissal, removal or reduction in rank of persons employed in civil capacities under Union or State: Provides two mandatory procedural safeguards.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: SAFEGUARDS: 311(1) Cannot be dismissed or removed by an authority SUBORDINATE to the appointing authority; 311(2) No dismissal, removal, or reduction in rank EXCEPT AFTER AN INQUIRY in which he is informed of charges and given a REASONABLE OPPORTUNITY OF BEING HEARD. 3 EXCEPTIONS TO INQUIRY (311(2) proviso): (a) Conviction on a criminal charge; (b) Where appointing authority records in writing that it is not reasonably practicable to hold inquiry; (c) Where President or Governor is satisfied that in the interest of SECURITY OF THE STATE it is not expedient to hold inquiry.
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Art 311 applies ONLY to civil servants; it does NOT apply to defence personnel or civilian employees of defence forces. Clause (c) security exception requires subjective satisfaction of President/Governor personally."	Polity::Articles
"Article 312 - PART XIV - SERVICES UNDER THE UNION AND THE STATES | CHAPTER I. -  SERVICES

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**

- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Resolution can be initiated ONLY in Rajya Sabha (federal safeguard protecting States' administrative autonomy). Requires 2/3rd present and voting."	Polity::Articles

"Article 317 - PART XIV - SERVICES UNDER THE UNION AND THE STATES | CHAPTER II. - PUBLIC SERVICE COMMISSIONS

**Definition:**
Removal and suspension of a member of a Public Service Commission: Chairman or member of UPSC or SPSC can be removed ONLY BY THE PRESIDENT.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: GROUNDS: (1) Proved misbehaviour: President refers matter to SUPREME COURT for inquiry under Art 145; if SC reports that member ought to be removed, President MUST remove (SC advice is binding); (2) Insolvent; (3) Paid employment outside office; (4) Infirmity of mind or body (no SC inquiry needed for 2, 3, 4).
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: SPSC members are APPOINTED by the Governor (Art 316), but can be REMOVED ONLY BY THE PRESIDENT (Governor CANNOT remove an SPSC member; Governor can only suspend pending inquiry)."	Polity::Articles

"Article 323A - PART XIVA - TRIBUNALS
Prompt: What does Article 323A provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Administrative Tribunals: Established by Parliament for public service disputes (CAT / SAT - 42nd Amdt)

**Definition:**
Administrative tribunals: Administrative Tribunals: PARLIAMENT MAY BY LAW establish administrative tribunals for adjudication of disputes regarding recruitment and conditions of service of public servants.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Inserted by 42nd Amendment Act, 1976. Parliament enacted Administrative Tribunals Act, 1985 creating Central Administrative Tribunal (CAT) and State Administrative Tribunals (SATs). Members have judicial/administrative background. L. Chandra Kumar v Union of India (1997): Supreme Court struck down exclusion of judicial review; decisions of CAT/SAT are subject to scrutiny before a Division Bench of the High Court under Article 226/227.
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Administrative Tribunals under Art 323A can be established ONLY BY PARLIAMENT (State Legislatures have no power under 323A). Litigants cannot approach Supreme Court directly from CAT; must first go to High Court Division Bench."	Polity::Articles
"Article 323B - PART XIVA - TRIBUNALS
Prompt: What does Article 323B provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Tribunals for Other Matters: Established by Parliament / State Legislature for taxation, tenancy, labor, elections

**Definition:**
Tribunals for other matters: Parliament or State Legislature may by law establish tribunals for: (a) Taxation; (b) Foreign exchange/customs; (c) Industrial and labour disputes; (d) Land reforms; (e) Urban property ceiling; (f) Elections to Parliament/Legislatures (excluding election petitions under 329); (g) Rent and tenancy.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Inserted by 42nd Amendment Act, 1976. Unlike 323A, tribunals under 323B can be created by BOTH Parliament and State Legislatures (within their legislative competence).
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: 323A is for public service matters ONLY and created ONLY by Parliament; 323B is for multiple subject-matters and can be created by BOTH Parliament and State Legislatures."	Polity::Articles
"Article 324 - PART XV - ELECTIONS
Prompt: What does Article 324 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Election Commission of India: Superintendence, direction, and control of Elections (CEC removal like SC Judge)

**Definition:**
Superintendence, direction and control of elections to be vested in an Election Commission: Conducts elections to Parliament, State Legislatures, President, and Vice-President.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: COMPOSITION: Chief Election Commissioner (CEC) + other Election Commissioners (ECs) appointed by President. Anoop Baranwal v Union of India (2023): SC directed appointment by committee of PM, CJI, and Leader of Opposition. Chief Election Commissioner and other Election Commissioners (Appointment, Conditions of Service and Term of Office) Act, 2023 replaced CJI with a Union Cabinet Minister. REMOVAL: CEC can be removed ONLY in like manner and on like grounds as an SC Judge (Art 124(4) special majority). Other ECs cannot be removed EXCEPT on the recommendation of the CEC.
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Election Commission of India does NOT conduct elections to Panchayats and Municipalities (conducted by State Election Commissions under Art 243K/243ZA). CEC has removal parity with SC Judge; other ECs do NOT have independent security of tenure (can be removed on CEC recommendation)."	Polity::Articles
"Article 325 - PART XV - ELECTIONS
Prompt: What does Article 325 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Single General Electoral Roll: No person ineligible on grounds ONLY of Religion, Race, Caste, or Sex

**Definition:**
No person to be ineligible for inclusion in, or to claim to be included in a special, electoral roll on grounds of religion, race, caste or sex.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Establishes a single, unified general electoral roll for every territorial constituency in India.
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Prohibits separate electorates based on religion or caste; secular basis of Indian democracy."	Polity::Articles
"Article 326 - PART XV - ELECTIONS
Prompt: What does Article 326 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Elections to Lok Sabha and State Assemblies on Basis of UNIVERSAL ADULT SUFFRAGE (Age lowered to 18 by 61st Amdt)

**Definition:**
Elections to the House of the People and to the Legislative Assemblies of States to be on the basis of adult suffrage: Elections to the House of the People and to the Legislative Assemblies of States to be on the basis of ADULT SUFFRAGE: Minimum voting age is 18 YEARS.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: 61st Constitutional Amendment Act, 1988 (operational 1989) lowered the voting age from 21 years to 18 years.
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Voting is a CONSTITUTIONAL/STATUTORY RIGHT under Art 326 and Section 62 of RPA 1951, NOT a Fundamental Right (PUCL case, Anoop Baranwal case)."	Polity::Articles
"Article 327 - PART XV - ELECTIONS
Prompt: What does Article 327 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Power of Parliament to Make Provision with respect to Elections to Legislatures (Delimitation Acts, RPA 1950/51)

**Definition:**
Power of Parliament to make provision with respect to elections to Legislatures: Enact laws for delimitation, electoral rolls, conduct of elections.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Parliament passed Representation of the People Act 1950, RPA 1951, and Delimitation Commission Acts under this power.
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Parliament has primary legislative competence for parliamentary and state assembly elections."	Polity::Articles
"Article 328 - PART XV - ELECTIONS
Prompt: What does Article 328 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Power of Legislature of a State to Make Provision with respect to Elections to such Legislature

Power of Legislature of a State to make provision with respect to elections to such Legislature, in so far as provision is not made by Parliament.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Subordinate/supplementary legislative power of States regarding state elections.
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: State legislation can operate only on matters not covered by parliamentary law."	Polity::Articles
"Article 329 - PART XV - ELECTIONS
Prompt: What does Article 329 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Bar to Interference by Courts in Electoral Matters: Delimitation immune; Election challenged ONLY by Election Petition

**Definition:**
Bar to interference by courts in electoral matters: Delimitation of constituencies or allotment of seats cannot be questioned in any court. No election can be called in question EXCEPT BY AN ELECTION PETITION presented to High Court (RPA 1951).

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Courts cannot interfere during the ongoing electoral process from notification to declaration of results (Mohinder Singh Gill case 1978).
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Judicial review is post-election only, via an election petition before the High Court."	Polity::Articles
"Article 329A - PART XV - ELECTIONS
Prompt: What does Article 329A provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** [Omitted] Special provision as to elections to Parliament in case of PM and Speaker (Repealed by 44th Amdt 1978)

**Definition:**
Special provision as to elections to Parliament in the case of Prime Minister and Speaker - [Omitted]: Special provision as to elections to Parliament in the case of Prime Minister and Speaker [Omitted].

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Status: [Omitted / Repealed]
- Key Provisions & Context: Inserted by 39th Amendment Act, 1975 during Emergency to immunise Indira Gandhi's election from judicial review. Core clause STRUCK DOWN in landmark Indira Nehru Gandhi v Raj Narain (1975) as violating free and fair elections and basic structure. Article omitted completely by 44th Amendment Act, 1978.
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: First case where a Constitutional Amendment was struck down using the Basic Structure doctrine."	Polity::Articles
"Article 330 - PART XVI - SPECIAL PROVISIONS RELATING TO CERTAIN CLASSES
Prompt: What does Article 330 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Reservation of Seats for Scheduled Castes and Scheduled Tribes in the House of the People (Lok Sabha)

**Definition:**
Reservation of seats for Scheduled Castes and Scheduled Tribes in the House of the People (Lok Sabha) in proportion to their population.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Currently 84 seats reserved for SCs and 47 seats reserved for STs in Lok Sabha (total 131 reserved seats out of 543).
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Reservation is strictly based on population proportion in each State/UT."	Polity::Articles

"Article 352 - PART XVIII - EMERGENCY PROVISIONS
Prompt: What does Article 352 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Proclamation of Emergency (National Emergency): War, External Aggression, Armed Rebellion; Written Cabinet advice

**Definition:**
Proclamation of Emergency (National Emergency): President may proclaim if satisfied that security of India or any part is threatened by WAR, EXTERNAL AGGRESSION, or ARMED REBELLION.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: 44th Amdt 1978: (1) Replaced 'internal disturbance' with 'ARMED REBELLION'; (2) President can issue proclamation ONLY on WRITTEN RECOMMENDATION OF THE CABINET (PM + Cabinet ministers; only place 'Cabinet' appears in Constitution); (3) Must be approved by BOTH Houses of Parliament by SPECIAL MAJORITY (majority of total + 2/3 present & voting) WITHIN 1 MONTH (was 2 months); (4) Stays in force for 6 MONTHS at a time (infinite extensions possible, 6 months each); (5) Revocation by President at any time OR if Lok Sabha passes a resolution disapproving it by SIMPLE MAJORITY (1/10th LS members can requisition special sitting). Imposed 3 times: 1962 (China), 1971 (Pakistan), 1975 (internal disturbance).
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Requires SPECIAL MAJORITY to approve/extend (special majority in both Houses); requires only SIMPLE MAJORITY of Lok Sabha alone to revoke."	Polity::Articles
"Article 353 - PART XVIII - EMERGENCY PROVISIONS
Prompt: What does Article 353 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Effect of Proclamation of Emergency: Union executive power extends to giving directions; Parliament legislates on State list

**Definition:**
Effect of Proclamation of Emergency: (a) Executive power of Union extends to giving directions to ANY State on manner of exercising executive power; (b) Parliament has power to make laws on State List subjects.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Federal structure transformed into a unitary system without formal constitutional amendment.
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: State Governments are NOT dismissed and State Legislatures are NOT dissolved; they remain in existence, but are subject to concurrent central legislative/executive control."	Polity::Articles

"Article 355 - PART XVIII - EMERGENCY PROVISIONS
Prompt: What does Article 355 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Duty of the Union to Protect States against External Aggression and Internal Disturbance

**Definition:**
Duty of the Union to protect States against external aggression and internal disturbance and to ensure that government of every State is carried on in accordance with provisions of Constitution.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**

- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Art 355 is the governing DUTY of the Union; Art 352 and 356 are the emergency POWERS triggered when that duty must be enforced."	Polity::Articles
"Article 356 - PART XVIII - EMERGENCY PROVISIONS
Prompt: What does Article 356 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Provisions in Case of Failure of Constitutional Machinery in States (President Rule / State Emergency; Bommai guidelines)

**Definition:**
Provisions in case of failure of constitutional machinery in States (President's Rule / State Emergency): President can assume functions of State Government if satisfied governance cannot be carried on in accordance with Constitution.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: Triggered on GOVERNOR'S REPORT OR SUO MOTU (Art 356(1)), or failure to comply with Union directions (Art 365). APPROVAL: Approved by BOTH Houses by SIMPLE MAJORITY WITHIN 2 MONTHS. Stays in force for 6 MONTHS at a time; MAXIMUM LIMIT = 3 YEARS. 44th Amdt: Beyond 1 year, can be extended only if: (a) National Emergency in operation; AND (b) Election Commission certifies elections cannot be held. S.R. BOMMAI CASE (1994): (1) Subject to judicial review; (2) Assembly cannot be dissolved until Parliament approves (only suspended animation); (3) Floor test is mandatory; (4) Secularism is basic structure; (5) Court can revive dissolved assembly if imposition held unconstitutional.
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Approved by SIMPLE MAJORITY (unlike Art 352 special majority); max 3 years (unlike Art 352 which has no max limit); State Government is dismissed and Assembly is dissolved/suspended."	Polity::Articles
"Article 357 - PART XVIII - EMERGENCY PROVISIONS

- Key Provisions & Context: President can delegate law-making power to Parliament or authorise expenditure from State Consolidated Fund pending sanction.

"Article 358 - PART XVIII - EMERGENCY PROVISIONS
Prompt: What does Article 358 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Suspension of Provisions of Article 19 during National Emergency (ONLY for War or External Aggression, NOT Armed Rebellion)

**Definition:**
Suspension of provisions of article 19 during emergencies: Suspension of provisions of Article 19 during emergencies: AUTOMATIC SUSPENSION of 6 freedoms under Article 19.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: CRITICAL 44TH AMENDMENT SAFEGUARDS: (1) Art 19 is suspended ONLY during emergency proclaimed on grounds of WAR or EXTERNAL AGGRESSION (EXTERNAL EMERGENCY); Art 19 is NOT suspended if emergency is on ground of Armed Rebellion (INTERNAL EMERGENCY); (2) Only laws containing a specific recital that they are in relation to emergency are protected from Art 19 challenge.
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Art 358 is AUTOMATIC (no presidential order needed), but applies ONLY to External Emergency (war/aggression), NEVER to Armed Rebellion."	Polity::Articles
"Article 359 - PART XVIII - EMERGENCY PROVISIONS
Prompt: What does Article 359 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Suspension of Enforcement of Fundamental Rights during Emergency (Except Articles 20 and 21 - 44th Amdt)

**Definition:**
Suspension of the enforcement of the rights conferred by Part III during emergencies: President may by order declare that the right to move any court for enforcement of specified FRs shall remain suspended.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: NON-AUTOMATIC (requires a specific Presidential Order specifying which rights are suspended). CRITICAL 44TH AMENDMENT EXCEPTION: The President CANNOT SUSPEND THE ENFORCEMENT OF ARTICLES 20 AND 21 UNDER ANY CIRCUMSTANCES. ADM Jabalpur (1976) was completely neutralized by 44th Amdt and later formally overruled in Puttaswamy (2017).
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Art 359 suspends the ENFORCEMENT of rights (remedial right under Art 32), not the substantive rights themselves. Articles 20 and 21 can NEVER be suspended."	Polity::Articles

"Article 368 - PART XX - AMENDMENT OF THE CONSTITUTION
Prompt: What does Article 368 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Power of Parliament to Amend Constitution and Procedure Therefor (Special Majority; Federal Ratification; Basic Structure Limit)

**Definition:**
Power of Parliament to amend the Constitution and procedure therefor: Parliament may in exercise of its constituent power amend by way of addition, variation or repeal any provision of the Constitution.

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: PROCEDURE: (1) Introduced in EITHER House (not in State Legislatures); (2) No prior Presidential recommendation needed; (3) Passed in EACH House by a SPECIAL MAJORITY: Majority of total membership + 2/3rd of members present and voting; (4) NO PROVISION FOR JOINT SITTING (each House must pass separately); (5) If it amends FEDERAL PROVISIONS (Arts 54, 55, 73, 162, 241, Part V Ch IV, Part VI Ch V, Part XI Ch I, 7th Schedule, representation in Parliament, Art 368), it must also be RATIFIED BY LEGISLATURES OF NOT LESS THAN HALF OF THE STATES by simple majority; (6) President MUST GIVE ASSENT (24th Amdt made assent mandatory). BASIC STRUCTURE DOCTRINE: Kesavananda Bharati v State of Kerala (1973) held constituent power is NOT unlimited; Parliament cannot destroy the 'Basic Structure' of the Constitution. Minerva Mills (1980) struck down Art 368(4)-(5) which claimed unlimited amendment power.
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: President CANNOT withhold assent and CANNOT return a Constitutional Amendment Bill (mandatory assent). No joint sitting exists. Simple majority amendments (Arts 2, 3, 169) are NOT deemed amendments under Art 368."	Polity::Articles

"Article 394 - PART XXII - SHORT TITLE, COMMENCEMENT, AUTHORITATIVE TEXT IN HINDI AND REPEALS
Prompt: What does Article 394 provide? What is its constitutional mandate, operational details, and high-yield UPSC exam points?"	"**Recall / Mnemonic:** Commencement: Articles 5-9, 60, 324, 366-367, 379, 380, 388, 391-393 on 26 Nov 1949; Remaining on 26 Jan 1950

**Definition:**
Commencement: Declares that the Constitution shall come into force on 26 JANUARY 1950 (Republic Day).

**Key Provisions & UPSC Traps:**
- Key Provisions & Context: CRITICAL PROVISO: Specified articles came into force IMMEDIATELY on 26 NOVEMBER 1949: Articles 5, 6, 7, 8, 9 (Citizenship), 60 (President's oath), 324 (Election Commission), 366, 367, 379, 380, 388, 391, 392, 393 and 394 itself.
- EXAM TRAP & UPSC DISTINCTION: Citizenship (5-9), President's oath (60), and Election Commission (324) came into force on 26 November 1949, NOT 26 January 1950."	Polity::Articles
"Article 394A - PART XXII - SHORT TITLE, COMMENCEMENT, AUTHORITATIVE TEXT IN HINDI AND REPEALS
